
﻿# Reconstruction Status Ledger

> Baseline: `4ec5b2c15`
> Program: [Ephyra Reconstruction Program](../ROADMAP.md)
> This ledger is append-oriented. Update it in the same commit as the work it describes.
> Forward plan: [2_0_COMPLETION_PLAN.md](2_0_COMPLETION_PLAN.md) · documentation index: [README.md](README.md)

## Status vocabulary

- `NOT_STARTED`: no implementation accepted.
- `IN_PROGRESS`: work exists but no phase gate is satisfied.
- `CODE_COMPLETE`: implementation and automated tests pass.
- `DEVICE_VERIFIED`: verified on declared physical/emulator matrix.
- `BLOCKED`: cannot proceed; blocker and owner are recorded.
- `ACCEPTED_DEBT`: consciously deferred with expiry/review condition.
- `VERIFIED`: all phase exit criteria satisfied.

## Evidence levels

Levels are defined by **what produces the evidence**, so a row is never expected to reach a level
this programme has no means to produce. See [`adr/0009`](adr/0009-evidence-channels-match-validation.md).

| Level | Evidence | Produced by |
|---|---|---|
| `E0` | claim only; no executable evidence |  |
| `E1` | static inspection | a reader |
| `E2` | JVM/Robolectric unit test | CI |
| `E3` | instrumentation, screenshot, or pointer-event test on an emulator | CI or a local emulator run |
| `E4-user` | validation on real user devices from a pushed build | the release/validation process |
| `E4-lab` | physical or representative device evidence produced in-tree | a deliberately set-up lab device |
| `E5` | performance measurement — manual/scripted capture (`B-051`: the `macrobenchmark` module is retired, D2) | a recorded measurement |

A task may be `CODE_COMPLETE` at `E2`. `DEVICE_VERIFIED` requires `E3` **plus** one of `E4-user` /
`E4-lab`, and each row declares which one it is waiting on.

Three rules follow, and they exist because their absence caused a fabricated evidence pass on
2026-09-25:

1. **A missing channel is a blocker, not a pass.** If a row needs a channel that does not exist, it is
   `BLOCKED` naming the channel. Never `pass`.
2. **An evidence record may only describe a run that occurred.** It names the build or commit, the
   device or device class, the exact steps, and the observed result. A record whose reproduction
   cannot be re-performed is void regardless of outcome.
3. **A claim that could regress silently again is gated at `E3`, not `E4-user`.** `E4-user` confirms
   behaviour; only a repeatable in-repository test guards it.

The other requires every declared feature-to-feature edge to be backed by a real import. **Falsified twice:** an unused `core:data` edge re-added to `feature/browse`, and a fabricated declared edge `:feature:upcoming -> :feature:webview` -- each turned the matching rule red naming the module. The fabricated edge was caught independently by the new rule *and* by the pre-existing staleness rule. Neither rule is "features must not depend on core:data" or "features must not depend on each other": the first is `B-027`/`DATA-001I`, and the second is already enforced by `feature modules do not depend on each other`. The two were separate needs conflated: the JVM suites legitimately use tiny thumbnails for speed, while the catalog describes the device-sized fixtures an `E4-lab` capture needs. `FixtureMaterialiser` now renders each catalog spec at exactly its declared geometry and commits the bytes (11 files, 1.46 MB), deterministically, so a re-run is byte-identical and re-derivable. **Animated GIF is deliberately excluded** â€” it is a reviewed immutable binary whose hash `SyntheticMediaFixturesTest` already protects, and re-encoding it would invalidate the identity that test exists to verify. | B-039 | **Attempted to reach the reader by seeding the local source; the blocker is ownership, not the fixture.** Followed `B-021` to its conclusion: discovered a real `source-local` module, read `LocalArchiveContentSource.getCatalog` (lists directories under the base dir) and `LocalSourceFileSystem`, then read the actual `storage_dir` out of the app DataStore rather than guessing â€” `file:///storage/emulated/0/Android/data/app.ephyra.dev/files/Ephyra`. Pushed the four committed device-sized fixtures as `E4-Lab-Series/chapter-001/00{1..4}.jpg` into the correct directory and confirmed the files are present and correctly named. **The local source still lists nothing.** Precise cause: `adb push` creates files owned by `shell`, and the app-owned sibling directories are `u0_a270`. The app cannot read a `shell`-owned tree under its own `Android/data` directory on API 37 â€” `run-as` is denied the same path, and `adb root` is refused because this is a production emulator build, so `chown` is not available either. **The correct fix is inside the app**: a dev/debug-gated seeding path that writes through its own `StorageManager`, or `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE` via the existing storage settings screen. Both are shippable-code decisions and therefore belong to the author, not to an agent poking at a device. All test directories were removed afterwards and the device tree is back to its original state. `E4-lab` for the reader remains BLOCKED on this, and no reader evidence is claimed. | **Seeder built and verified on device.** `app/src/debug` (debug source set only, so absent from nightly/release) now holds `E4LabFixtureSeeder` + a manifest-declared `E4LabFixtureReceiver` triggered by `adb shell am broadcast -a ephyra.app.debug.SEED_E4LAB_FIXTURES -n app.ephyra.dev/ephyra.app.debug.E4LabFixtureReceiver`. It writes the committed fixtures through the app's own `StorageManager`, so they are app-owned. **Verified on `emulator-5554`:** after the broadcast, `E4-Lab Series/chapter-001/001..006.jpg` exist owned by `u0_a270` at exactly the committed byte sizes (250680, 187605, 197651, 19607, 19607, 851161). The `B-039` ownership blocker is therefore solved. `E4LabAssetParityTest` guards the staged copies against drift and is **falsification-verified** â€” flipping one byte of a staged asset turns it red, restoring turns it green; the corruption was same-length precisely so a size check could not have caught it. **Still BLOCKED on a second, separate issue:** the Local source screen renders a perpetual spinner rather than the seeded series, with no app exception in logcat. So the content is on disk and correctly owned, but the browse surface does not surface it. That is a distinct defect from the ownership problem and is not claimed as fixed. |
| B-036 | **Housekeeping audit, 2026-09-26, after 18 commits. Asked whether the codebase can be simplified, and the honest answer separates three groups that look identical in an "unused" grep.** (a) **2.0 target contracts â€” keep, despite zero consumers.** `DocumentTilePartition`, `DownloadArtifactIndex`, `DownloadArtifactVerifier` and `UniFileDownloadArtifactProbe` are unreferenced from production *and* tests, yet all four are named in this ledger as target architecture. Deleting them as dead code would destroy verified 2.0 work â€” the exact mistake ADR-0008 exists to prevent. (b) **Genuinely ambiguous legacy utilities â€” flagged, not deleted.** `core/download/util/ChapterFilter.kt`, `ChapterUtils.kt` and `ChapterGetNextUnread.kt` have no consumers and no ledger entry, but they came from ordinary feature commits rather than the contract-first programme, so they are pre-existing debt, not something this work created. Removing them belongs to whoever owns that module. (c) **One real redundancy: `ci.yml` and `build.yml` both gate pull requests and both run the whole unit-test suite.** `ci.yml` runs `testDebugUnitTest ktlintCheck --continue`; `build.yml` runs `spotlessCheck` in `quality` and `testDebugUnitTest` again in `build`. Every PR therefore executes all 1,677 tests twice, and `ci.yml` invokes `ktlintCheck`, a linter this project does not otherwise use â€” it has `spotlessCheck` in its pre-commit hook. Recorded rather than deleted: removing a gate is a decision, and deleting the wrong one would leave a pull-request path with no verification at all. | OPEN â€” one real redundancy found; consolidation is not my call alone |
| B-043 | **The Macrobenchmark workflow has never succeeded: 24 consecutive scheduled runs, all red, since 2026-09-18 or earlier.** Found 2026-09-27 by reading the scheduled run list rather than waiting for a PR, because the workflow triggers on `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` only, so its failure is invisible to anyone reviewing a change. All three matrix jobs fail at the same step, "Run macrobenchmark on emulator". **Root cause established by local reproduction, not inference.** The CI log endpoint returns 403 without auth, so the error was obtained by running it here: `:macrobenchmark:assembleBenchmark` succeeds, and `:macrobenchmark:connectedBenchmarkAndroidTest` reports `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` while the test itself fails -- `tests="1" failures="1"`, `org.junit.AssumptionViolatedException` at `MacrobenchmarkRule.kt:210`, logcat `assumption failed: scrollLibrary`. `test-result-exit-code.txt` reads `0`, so the build is green twice over while nothing was measured. This is the macrobenchmark framework refusing to measure on an unrooted emulated device, not a defect in the benchmark sources. **Two separate lessons.** A scheduled workflow that always fails is indistinguishable from one that is ignored, and it had been red for 24 days. And `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` from a connected test task does not mean the test passed -- the same class as the missing `testInstrumentationRunner` reported in `B-030` and the `UP-TO-DATE` no-op in `B-041`, and the reason every claim in this programme is checked against the result XML rather than the build line. **Fix is an owner decision** because it costs either CI minutes (a rooted/userdebug image) or hardware (a physical device on manual dispatch), and the alternative is to state plainly in the workflow that it needs hardware. | RESOLVED 2026-11-07 (`B-051` + D2): the workflow was deleted by the owner decision, and the `macrobenchmark` module itself is retired by D2; the budget it could never measure is a stated manual target |
| B-045 | **Three near-identical copies of "next unread chapter" existed; one was dead and has been deleted 2026-09-27.** Found by tracing `DEF-007`'s navigation behaviour rather than by a dead-code sweep, so the reason it matters is that the copies *disagreed*. `app/util/chapter/ChapterGetNextUnread.kt`, `ChapterFilterDownloaded.kt`, and `ChapterRemoveDuplicates.kt` were each a near-verbatim copy of a `core/download/util` counterpart, differing only in taking `DownloadCache` where the live versions take `DownloadManager`. Verified zero references outside their own files â€” nothing in `app`, in any test source set, or in any other module imported them, and the package had no same-package callers either, so no import would have been needed to have found a use. `getNextUnread` in particular was duplicated a fourth time as a `private` helper in `MangaViewModel:837`, but that one is **not** a duplicate: it prioritises a chapter that is in progress (`lastPageRead > 0`) and so behaves differently by design, which is what its two tests assert. Deleting the three dead files is behaviour-preserving; `:app:assembleDebug` and the full `:app` suite pass after removal, and the compiler is the proof â€” an import count can miss a reflection or DI path, a build cannot. **The risk this row records is not the dead code, it is the divergence**: `removeDuplicates` groups by `it.chapterNumber`, which is a `Double` compared by `groupBy` on boxed equality, so a restore-widened `Float` value lands in its own group and the chapter is not deduplicated against its twin. That is the same `DEF-012` defect class one layer out, in a path this session has not yet audited. It is recorded here rather than as a deferred design question, because **the live `removeDuplicates` in `core/download/util/ChapterUtils.kt` was itself keyed on boxed `Double` equality** â€” and that one is production code, consumed by `ReaderViewModel` at both the navigation list and the download-ahead list. See `DEF-013`. |

| DEF-013 | Skip-duplicate navigation could reach the same chapter twice after a database round trip, because duplicate grouping compared chapter numbers by exact `Double` equality. | Found 2026-09-27 while tracing `DEF-007`, then **fixed and falsification-verified**. The live `removeDuplicates` did `groupBy { it.chapterNumber }`. That is exact bit comparison on boxed `Double`s, so a restored chapter holding `12.300000190734863` and a live chapter holding `12.3` landed in **different groups** â€” proven, not inferred: a probe asserted one group and got `expected: <1> but was: <2>`. The chapter was therefore never deduplicated against its twin, and with `skipDupe` enabled `ReaderViewModel` could build a navigation list containing both. This is the same `DEF-012` root cause one layer out, in a path the `DEF-012` audit did not cover because that audit swept comparisons and this was a *grouping key*. **Fix:** grouping now goes through a tolerant key, so there is one chapter-number rule in the app rather than two that can drift. `core:download` had **no test source set at all**, so this behaviour had never been exercised; it now has one, and `RemoveDuplicatesTest` (6 tests) pins the restored-twin case, exact duplicates, genuinely different numbers staying apart, unrecognised numbers never collapsing (a source reporting no numbers would otherwise collapse a whole series), and the current-chapter-wins preference order. **Falsified:** reverting the grouping key turns exactly 4 of the 6 red, including the defect case. Unobserved on a device â€” reachability is established from the declared `Float`/`Double` boundary and the arithmetic, as with `DEF-012`. | CODE_COMPLETE at E2, production-wired, falsification-verified. Unobserved on a device. |
| DEF-014 | A chapter removed upstream silently kept its stale read, bookmark and fetch date after a database round trip, because three `TreeSet<Double>` membership tests compared chapter numbers by exact bits. | Found 2026-09-27 by the behaviour-based sweep that followed `DEF-013`, and it is the **third and last** shape the `Float`-widening defect takes. `SyncChaptersWithSource` built `deletedChapterNumbers`, `deletedReadChapterNumbers` and `deletedBookmarkedChapterNumbers` as `TreeSet<Double>` from `removedChapters` (source-side values) and then tested the **database-side** chapter against them. A restored value is `Float`-widened, so `12.3 !in deletedChapterNumbers` is `true` and the chapter returned early at the `!in` guard, keeping whatever read/bookmark state the database still had for a chapter that no longer exists upstream. It also missed the `dateFetch` carry-over, so a removed-and-restored chapter would reappear in the Updates tab as newly fetched. **Proven, not inferred:** a probe built the real `TreeSet`, inserted `12.3`, and asked whether it contained the widened value â€” `expected: <true> but was: <false>`. **Correction to an earlier claim in this row, 2026-09-27:** I first wrote that a probe had found whole numbers are *not* exact through a `Float` round trip, and used that to justify re-examining `GenerateAuthorityChapters`. **That was wrong, and it was my error rather than the probe's.** The probe output had `-1` for "first inexact integer in 1..100000", meaning *no* inexact value was found in that range; I misread it as a found failure. A second probe located the real boundary: the first inexact integer is **16777217**, i.e. `2^24 + 1`, exactly where a `Float`'s 24-bit significand runs out. So whole numbers *are* exact, and `GenerateAuthorityChapters`'s `Set<Double>` membership is safe for any realistic `totalChapters` â€” it was correctly left alone. The lesson is the same one that produced the other rows here: a probe result must be read, not pattern-matched, and a claim written into the ledger is a claim that will be inherited. | CODE_COMPLETE at E2, production-wired, falsification-verified. Unobserved on a device. |
| DEF-015 | The two chapter-sync decisions that had no tests at all now have them, and the `DEF-014` decision is pinned where it is made rather than only where its rule is defined. | Added 2026-09-27. `ShouldUpdateDbChapter` (the `DEF-012` fix) and `SyncChaptersWithSource` (the `DEF-014` fix) were both untested. `ShouldUpdateDbChapter` is a pure predicate with no collaborators, so it is tested directly: `ShouldUpdateDbChapterTest` (8) covers the restored-number case, each of the five fields that legitimately trigger a rewrite, and the unrecognised-number guard. **Falsified** by restoring the exact `!=` comparison, which turns **exactly 2 of 641** red -- and the second is a finding rather than a restatement: under `!=`, two unrecognised numbers compared *equal*, so a source reporting no chapter numbers would have had its rows silently treated as unchanged. That failure mode was invisible until the guard test existed. `SyncChaptersWithSource` has ten injected ports and no test source set, and building ten fakes to reach one decision would have been scaffolding for its own sake, so the `DEF-014` decision was **extracted** into `RemovedChapterState` instead: `from(removedChapters)` builds what is inheritable, `inherit(candidate, state)` answers for one candidate and returns null when there is nothing to inherit. That is a genuine design improvement independent of testing -- the decision was previously a 25-line inline block interleaved with the fetch-date bookkeeping it exists to support. `RemovedChapterStateTest` (7) covers the defect case directly, plus that inheritance is not sticky (a replacement of something unread stays unread), that the most recently fetched removed chapter wins a shared number, and that an unrecognised candidate inherits nothing. **Falsified** by replacing the bucketed keys with exact bit keys, which turns **exactly 1 of 648** red -- the restored-candidate case. The interactor itself is still untested at the call site, and that gap is recorded rather than glossed. | CODE_COMPLETE at E2. Unobserved on a device. |
| DEF-016 | The `EPSILON` introduced with the `DEF-012` fix was itself too small: a fixed absolute tolerance stops working at chapter 4096, so every `DEF-012`/`013`/`014` fix was still broken for any series numbered in the thousands. | Found 2026-09-27 by probing the *bound* of the rule rather than its behaviour. A `Float` has 24 bits of significand, so its relative error is fixed and its **absolute** error grows with the magnitude of the value. `EPSILON = 1e-4` covers chapter 12.3 comfortably, but at chapter 131072 the worst `Float` round-trip error is `0.00625` â€” 62 times larger. Measured exhaustively, a fixed `1e-4` **first fails at chapter 4096**. My own docstring had claimed the widest error was "about `1.2e-5` at chapter 10,000"; the real figure is roughly 8x that, and the claim was never measured. **Fix:** `ChapterNumber.tolerance(magnitude)` takes the larger of the `1e-4` floor and two units in the last place at that magnitude (`2^-23`), and `sameChapterNumber`, `hasReached` and `bucket` all route through it so a set key and a comparator cannot disagree. `bucket` previously divided by the fixed `EPSILON`, so it would have bucketed a widened `131072.1` differently from its twin. Verified by exhaustive probe across chapters 1..200000 with fractional parts: the scaled rule matched **every** round trip while merging **zero** distinct chapters. `ChapterNumberTest` gains 5 tests including a 1..2000 exhaustive round-trip sweep and a matching no-merge sweep. **Falsified** by restoring the fixed `1e-4`, which turns exactly 2 of 652 red â€” `a large chapter number still round trips` and `bucket agrees with sameChapterNumber at large magnitudes`. Also corrects the `DEF-014` row above, which asserted a probe had found whole numbers inexact through a `Float`; the probe output was `-1` (none found in 1..100000) and I misread it. The first genuinely inexact integer is 16777217, so `GenerateAuthorityChapters` is safe and was correctly left alone. | CODE_COMPLETE at E2, falsification-verified. Unobserved on a device. |
| DEF-017 | The `Float` is not a backup artefact. Chapter numbers are narrowed on *every* database write, so the `DEF-012`/`013`/`014`/`015` rows all name the wrong trigger. | Found 2026-09-27 by asking why the value is a `Float` at all rather than only where a backup declared it. The chain is: the source model `SChapter.chapter_number` is a `Float`; `Chapter.toSChapter`/`toDbChapter` narrows the domain `Double` with `.toFloat()`; the legacy entity column `ChapterImpl.chapter_number` is declared `Float`; and the read path widens it back with `chapter_number.toDouble()`. So `12.3` becomes `12.300000190734863` **the first time a chapter is saved**, for every user, with no backup and no restore involved â€” proven by probe, not inferred. Meanwhile `ChapterEntity` (the Room entity) declares `chapterNumber: Double`, so the two persistence paths in the same app disagree about the type of the same column. **Why this matters more than the four defects it explains:** every one of those rows, and every commit message describing them, tells a maintainer the failure needs a restore to appear. That is wrong, and it would lead someone to conclude the population at risk is "people who have restored a backup" rather than "everyone with a decimal chapter number". The behaviour is identical in both cases â€” the backup `Float` is simply one more place the same narrowing happens. **What is not fixed here:** widening the column is a schema migration, and `DATA-001` (schema policy) is `NOT_STARTED`, so the type change is a Phase 12 decision rather than something to slip in beside a behaviour fix. The tolerance in `ChapterNumber` is what makes the existing narrowing *correct* rather than what causes it. Recorded so the migration has the finding, and so no future pass repeats the wrong trigger. | **Corrected again 2026-09-27, and the correction removes work rather than adding it.** I had recorded that the loss happened on "every database write" and that `DATA-001` was therefore a schema migration. Both wrong, and checking rather than assuming is what caught it. `ChapterRepositoryImpl` -- the repository bound in `AppModule` -- uses `ChapterDao` and `ChapterEntity`, and `ChapterEntity.chapterNumber` is **already `Double`**. The `Float` entity `ChapterImpl` is legacy and is not on the live path. **The actual narrow point is the source model:** `SChapter.chapter_number` is a `Float`, and `ContentSourceAdapter`, `DynamicHttpSource`, `LocalSource` and `MigrateMangaUseCase` all assign `.toFloat()` into it. `Chapter.fromSChapter` widens it back, and `ChapterRecognition.parseChapterNumber` *returns* that widened value whenever it is already recognised, so the name-based re-parse is only a fallback for unrecognised numbers. The consequence is that the loss **is** real for every online chapter with a decimal number -- but it happens at the source boundary, not in storage, and **`DATA-001` is therefore not a migration.** No schema change, no data rewrite, no version bump. The decision reduces to whether to widen `SChapter.chapter_number` to `Double` (a source-api contract change touching every source) or to keep the tolerant `ChapterNumber` policy that already compensates correctly. That is a materially smaller and better-understood piece of work than the one I queued, and the earlier claim should not have been made without tracing which repository is bound. |
| B-046 | **`ReaderReadCompletionWiringTest` was order-dependent: its mocks are class-level `val`s that were never cleared, so `coVerify(exactly = 1)` counted calls across the whole class.** Found 2026-09-27, and it is a defect in a test written earlier in this session to close `DEF-011`. Two tests expect exactly one read write (`reaching the genuinely last page` and `a merge that absorbed the tail`); whichever ran second saw two and failed. JUnit does not guarantee method order, so which one fails varies between runs. **How it presented, and why the diagnosis was not obvious at first:** the class passed every time it was run alone with `--tests`, and failed only inside a multi-module run where the ordering differed â€” so it looked like a regression from whatever had just changed. I did not assume that: I stashed the working changes and ran the class in isolation at `HEAD`, where it passed, then restored the changes and ran it again, where it also passed. Two identical outcomes with a different answer means the variable is neither the code nor the invocation, and the remaining candidate is run-to-run ordering. Fixed by `clearMocks` in `setUp`. Verified by three consecutive full `:feature:reader` runs rather than one, because a single green run cannot distinguish "fixed" from "passed this time". **The general lesson, which is the same one behind the inert `ARC-001` gate and the two count-baseline workflows:** a test whose correctness depends on state it does not own is not a test, it is a coin flip that usually lands green. |


| B-040 | **The nightly build was not missing a secret â€” the workflow never passed it. 2026-09-26.** After the owner configured `EPHYRA_NIGHTLY_STORE_PASSWORD` and `EPHYRA_NIGHTLY_KEY_PASSWORD`, `Nightly Build` run #453 still failed at `Build app`. Chasing the actual cause rather than assuming the secret was wrong: the `Build app` step in `nightly.yml` had **no `env:` block at all**. GitHub does not export repository secrets into a step's environment implicitly, so `signingSecret()` could never see them â€” the build could not succeed however the secrets were configured. Added the `env:` mapping to the two `EPHYRA_`-prefixed names that `signingSecret()` derives. **Verified by reproducing the CI command locally with those variables set**: `./gradlew assembleNightly -Penable-updater -PversionCode=453` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 38s`, 5 APKs written, and `apksigner verify` reports `CN=Ephyra` â€” so the secret genuinely reached the build rather than being assumed to. `B-035` is therefore **resolved by a code fix, not by configuration**: no further secret work is needed, and `B-017` (the keystore still tracked in git) remains open as its own decision. | **CLOSED on CI** â€” `Nightly Build` #455 `success`; `Build app` and `Sign APK` both green; release `nightly` published 2026-09-26T16:56:06Z with 5 APKs (`app-arm64-v8a-nightly-f58a81d.apk` and the armeabi-v7a/universal/x86/x86_64 siblings). Locally `apksigner verify` reports `CN=Ephyra`, so the signing key is the real nightly key, not the debug fallback. `B-017` remains open separately. |
| B-041 | **Pinch and double-tap could not be driven through `adb shell input`, 2026-09-26. RESOLVED 2026-09-26.** One pointer per process invocation, so two taps could not land inside the 350 ms double-tap window and no pinch could be produced at all. Discovered attempting an `E4-lab` transform capture on a live reader. | **RESOLVED.** The limit was the *tool*, not the platform. `PagerGestureInjectTest` injects real `MotionEvent`s through Compose, on a real device, into the real `pagerGestureStream` â€” so multi-touch is now drivable and `DEF-001` has device evidence for the transform gesture itself, not only for a static layer. **Measured on API 37:** scale `1.0 -> 5.0`, raster `1080x2424 -> 5400x12120` (exactly 5x), 28 arbiter effects. **Falsified at the real defect seam:** replacing `pagerZoomLayer(state.transform)` with an identity layer left the scale moving but the raster unchanged at `1080x2424`, and the assertion reported *"A changed scale with an unchanged raster is the exact DEF-001 defect: state moved and nothing drew."* **Two harness bugs found on the way, both mine and both instructive.** The host initially copied only the transform and never set `viewportSize`, so the viewport stayed permanently unmeasured and all 25 `TransformUpdated` effects correctly no-opped â€” the product was right and the harness wrong, which is why the assertion names the effect count. And writing frames to a relative path put them on the *device*, not the host; worse, the write ran first and masked the gesture assertions entirely, so a broken artifact path presented as a broken gesture. Frames now go to external app storage and all assertions precede any write. **Frames are not durable:** Gradle uninstalls both APKs at the end of a connected run, so the measurements are logged to the preserved logcat rather than kept as images. |
| B-042 | **No test or CI step depends on a live third-party extension repository, verified 2026-09-26 at the owner's request.** The `keiyoushi.github.io` reference in `build.yml` is a string inside an error message about the okhttp-zstd pin, not a fetch; no test source set names it; no CI step installs extensions; and the app hardcodes no default extension repository (the URL comes from user data via a deep link). All 260 test source files were enumerated and every external host they name is fixture data (`example.com`, `a.test`, `192.168.1.100`, `mangadex.org` as sample strings) â€” none is dialled. Added `ExternalServiceDependencyTest` so this cannot regress: it fails on a repository-index URL aimed at a non-local, non-reserved host, and on a hardcoded community extension repo. | **CLOSED by a gate, and the gate is falsified.** A planted `https://keiyoushi.github.io/.../index.json` in a test source made both rules fail with file, line and host; removing it turned them green. The rule is structural, not a count, so a hundred such dependencies would not weaken it and retiring one needs no edit. |
| B-030 | **Found 2026-09-26: `feature:reader` had no `testInstrumentationRunner`, and the failure mode is the dangerous kind.** Instrumentation failed with `INSTRUMENTATION_FAILED: .../androidx.test.runner.AndroidJUnitRunner` and Gradle reported the task **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** with `tests="0"`. A green device task that ran nothing reads as evidence and is worse than a red one. Declared `testInstrumentationRunner` to match `:app`, and the module now reports its real test count. **Second finding: I attempted a `DEF-003` painted-bounds test and could not make it trustworthy, so it was deleted rather than shipped.** The claim is that a per-item transform makes a slice paint outside its layout slot. Moving the transform back onto the item did produce a visibly different raster â€” `[0, bg, bg, 1, bg, bg, 2]` against `[0, 1, 2, 3, 4]` at fit â€” so the defect reproduces and the measurement technique works. But the *same* gap signature also appeared with the **correct** container transform, including after replacing `LazyColumn` with a fully-composed `Column`. That means the background gaps come from the transform-origin/clip geometry of my harness, not from item placement, so a test built on them would have been asserting my own scaffolding. Shipping a test that cannot distinguish the two placements would be a false claim on a user-reported defect. `DEF-003` therefore stays at `E2`. The technique â€” scan a single column for maximal same-colour runs and assert order, uniqueness and absence of gaps â€” is sound and reusable; the harness needs the real `ComposeWebtoonReader` geometry, not a stand-in. | OPEN â€” technique proven, harness not yet sound; `DEF-003` stays `E2` |
| B-031 | **Evidence audit of every `DEF` row, 2026-09-26.** Done because I had been adding evidence requirements only to the rows I happened to be working on, which is cherry-picking â€” the same defect class as an inert gate, in milder form. I checked each row's named evidence and whether the policy it names actually has a production consumer outside `core:domain`. **Two rows were overstating and are corrected, and one apparent finding was my own error:** `DEF-005` **turned out to be correctly wired, and this row was wrong** â€” see `B-033`. I searched for the `MotionPolicy` class name and missed that `Motion.sharedElementContainerDuration()` is the real path, so the original downgrade was a false positive from a flawed method, not a real defect; `DEF-006` claimed a "DAO contract + ViewModel test" as evidence and **no such test exists anywhere**, downgraded to `NOT_STARTED`; `DEF-007` claimed `CODE_COMPLETE` while `ReaderChapterWindowPolicy` has zero production consumers, downgraded to `IN_PROGRESS` with its 13 tests acknowledged as real but unused. **Two rows verified sound:** `DEF-008` (8 Robolectric tests with a real JPEG round-trip, previously falsification-verified) and `DEF-009`, whose evidence was only "pure resolver contract tests" and is now named as `DefaultReadingModeResolverTest` (8, green) with its `ReaderViewModel` consumer recorded. `DEF-001`/`002` at `E3` and `DEF-003` at `E2` re-checked and unchanged. **Net effect after the `B-033` correction: `DEF-006` moved to `IN_PROGRESS` (behaviour correct, guarantee absent), `DEF-007` moved to `IN_PROGRESS` (behaviour correct, policy unwired), `DEF-005` reinstated to `CODE_COMPLETE`. No row was downgraded to `NOT_STARTED` in the end.** The honest headline is that a third of the `DEF` ledger asserted wiring that did not exist, and no test would ever have caught that â€” only reading the tree did. | AUDITED â€” 3 rows corrected, 2 verified, no row advanced |
| B-052 | **The webtoon memory plan was built on two wrong numbers, both mine.** I first claimed a "250-slice webtoon = 2.06 GB" of decoded bitmaps, then recanted to ~73 MB, then found the original claim's mechanism does not apply. Checked rather than assumed: (1) `ReaderPage.mergedBitmap` is **never assigned anywhere in `viewer/webtoon/`** â€” only `PagerViewer` and `Downloader` set it â€” so the `asImageBitmap()` read I based the claim on cannot execute in webtoon mode; (2) `SlicedWebtoonImage` holds decoded slices in `produceState`, which is **composition-scoped**, so `LazyColumn` disposes them when the item leaves the window; (3) `MAX_SLICES = 32` caps slices per page, so a long strip is several pages, not 250 slices of one. **What actually scales with scroll distance is the encoded `ReaderPage.cachedBytes`,** held until `releasePageResources()` at chapter disposal â€” roughly 73 MB for a 250-slice chapter, which exceeds the 64 MiB budget. That term is real and is what `PageByteStore` addresses; the 2 GB figure was decoration. **Recorded because the near-miss is the lesson:** twice I asserted a magnitude from arithmetic on an unverified premise, and a plan built on it would have routed webtoon through Coil and dropped `mergedBitmap` â€” churn on the reader's hottest path for a defect that is not there. |
| B-055 | **Whole-image-pipeline audit, 2026-09-29: five defects fixed, the rest recorded rather than fixed.** Audited in four segments (Coil loader + cover store, reader page loaders + byte store, download/save/local-file, decode/transform/memory) and **every finding was verified against the code before anything was changed** â€” the sub-audits produced both real defects and over-claims, and the over-claims were discarded rather than reported. **Fixed:** `BoundedPageByteStore` was not thread-safe while its callers are not confined (IO decode threads put concurrently, Main pins, the view-model scope disposes), so the budget counter drifted and `clear` could throw out of chapter disposal; `ImageUtil.canUseHardwareBitmap` failed **open** on an unparseable header (`maxOf(-1,-1) <= threshold`), admitting unknown-size sources to the GPU path; the legacy cover migration called `DiskCache.remove` while its Snapshot was open, which Coil's own `openSnapshot` contract says cannot delete the entry, so the duplicate survived, the migration re-ran forever, and the un-closed snapshot also blocked the trim; `writeResponseToCoverCache` used `peekBody(Long.MAX_VALUE)`, buffering a whole "cover" into the heap on a fetcher pool eight wide; and `Downloader` discarded the result of the publish `renameTo` and then indexed the chapter and marked it DOWNLOADED, so a SAF-backed rename failure produced a chapter recorded as downloaded whose directory does not exist. **Recorded, not fixed, each with its reason:** (i) `MEDIA_PIPELINE_CONTRACT.md` mandates a `DecodePlan` in cache identity, a typed retryable decode error and an observable fallback reason â€” **`DecodePlanner`, `DecodeReason`, `DecodePlan.cacheKey`, `PageImage` and `PageLoadFailure` have no production callers at all**, so the live key (`readerPageMemoryCacheKey`) carries none of the plan's fields. This is `B-032` again in the media cluster, and the same shape ADR-0008's contract-first exception was flagged for: a verified, tested, unconsumed surface. Wiring it is a real change to the decode path, not something to do inside a bug fix. (ii) The verification the download contract requires is **dead code**: `verifyDownloadArtifact` and `UniFileDownloadArtifactProbe` are referenced only from `src/test`, while production `isDownloadSuccessful` compares two integer counts and never reads a byte â€” and `getImageExtension` falls back to `jpg` for a body that is not an image, so an HTML error page satisfies both counts. (iii) `postProcessPages` discards its `renameTo` result and is never re-verified, so two pages can vanish from a chapter that still publishes. (iv) `BorderCropTransformation.cacheKey` is dead on every production path because both readers override the key with `readerPageMemoryCacheKey`, so bumping the algorithm version keeps serving old pixels â€” a versioning mechanism that cannot invalidate. (v) `ArchivePageLoader` re-opens and linearly rescans the archive per page read, O(nÂ²) per chapter; inherited from upstream Mihon. (vi) `GLUtil.DEVICE_TEXTURE_LIMIT` reads `EGL_MAX_PBUFFER_WIDTH`, a pbuffer attribute rather than the GL texture ceiling, so the hardware threshold is not the limit the guard claims to enforce; also inherited, and changing it would alter decode behaviour on real devices without a measurement. **Not claimed:** nothing in this pass has been compiled or executed â€” no JDK, no Gradle cache, no Android SDK â€” and the new tests are written to be run, not run. **A second pass closed three more of the small items:** `postProcessPages` deleted both source pages and then renamed the merged file unchecked, inside a `catch` that deliberately swallows merge errors, so a failed rename published a chapter with two pages missing and no error anywhere â€” a new `PageLostException` separates the unrecoverable case from the tolerated one, so the download fails instead; `PageByteStoreOwner.retain` recorded its index maps even when the store *declined* the bytes, so `pageCount` counted a page whose bytes were gone and `byId` held a strong reference to it for the life of the chapter â€” the bounded store's retention reached through the back door; and `CoverCache.pruneOldCovers` measured its budget with `listFiles().sumOf { it.length() }`, where a directory's `length()` is its own inode size, so the `custom/` subtree counted as ~4 KiB and the cap never saw a single custom cover. **A finding about the audit itself is recorded too:** the first version of that pruning test asserted only that a custom cover survives pruning, which the *old* code also satisfies, because custom covers are not prunable in either version â€” a blind gate of exactly the kind `B-054` records. It was rewritten so the two implementations must disagree (64 KiB custom + 8 KiB unprotected remote against a 60 KiB budget: the flat sum sees 12 KiB and prunes nothing, the recursive sum sees 72 KiB and prunes the remote), because a test that cannot tell the fix from the bug is worse than no test. | `BoundedPageByteStore.kt`, `PageByteStoreOwner.kt`, `ImageUtil.kt`, `MangaCoverFetcher.kt`, `Downloader.kt`; `BoundedPageByteStoreTest` (+2) | FIXED â€” 8 defects; 6 recorded above |
| B-057 | **The build ran two Kotlin compilers at once â€” a Compose compiler at 2.4.20 against a serialization compiler and `buildSrc` at 2.3.20 â€” and nothing caught it, because a compiler/runtime split is exactly the defect that does not fail the build.** `gradle/libs.versions.toml` declared `kotlin = 2.4.20` and `gradle/kotlinx.versions.toml` declared `kotlin_version = 2.3.20`. The two names looked like one setting recorded twice, and the reason they are not is that **`kotlin_version` is the one that reaches the toolchain**: it feeds the serialization *compiler* plugin (`kotlinx.versions.toml:36`), `buildSrc`'s own `kotlin-gradle-plugin` (`buildSrc/build.gradle.kts:7`), and the root buildscript classpath, while `kotlin` in `libs.versions.toml` only ever fed the Compose compiler plugin (`libs.versions.toml:128`). Separately, `kotlin-stdlib` was being force-upgraded to **2.4.10** by `firebase-crashlytics` 3.0.8, so the runtime was a *third* version. Fixed in `d6603ed56` by aligning `kotlin_version` to 2.4.20. **Why the evidence is a resolution dump and not a green build:** `buildEnvironment` reported the serialization plugin at 2.3.20 before and 2.4.20 after, which is the only view from outside that distinguishes the two catalogs, since a successful build is equally consistent with either. **Target chosen deliberately:** 2.4.20 is the newest *stable* Kotlin â€” 2.5.0-Beta1 exists in Maven Central and was not taken. KSP stays at 2.3.12, which is already the newest published KSP and pairs with the 2.4.x compiler; `:app:kspDebugKotlin` running clean is the real test of that pairing, because KSP is the component most likely to break on a compiler bump. **Not a build file, and worth knowing:** the residual `kotlin-stdlib:{strictly 2.4.10}` in the resolution graph is emitted by the Kotlin Gradle Plugin itself. A search for `strictly`, `force`, and `kotlin.stdlib` across every `*.kts` in the repo returns nothing, so there is no local pin to chase and the version difference is not drift anyone introduced. **Left in place deliberately:** the value is still duplicated across two catalogs, because a version catalog cannot read another catalog's `versions` block and collapsing `kotlinx.versions.toml` into `libs.versions.toml` would mean restructuring both `settings.gradle.kts` files. That is real debt with a real recurrence risk, so the file now carries a comment stating that the two must move together. A structural gate asserting the two agree would be the durable fix and is not written. |
| B-061 | **The `build.yml` layering violation is resolved properly: the crop cache key is now one definition in `core:domain`, referenced by both sides, rather than an import that inverts layering or a second literal that drifts.** Closes the decision `B-060` recorded as unresolved. **The shape of the conflict.** `core:data` owns `BorderCropTransformation`; `feature:reader` owns `readerPageMemoryCacheKey`, which sets Coil's `memoryCacheKey` and therefore *replaces* the key Coil would have computed from the transformation's own `cacheKey`. So the reader must fold in the crop *algorithm version*, and the only value that carries it was `BorderCropTransformation.CACHE_KEY` in `core:data`. Importing it inverted the layering and failed the gate; copying the string reintroduced the drift class this commit was written to remove. **The resolution.** `core:domain` is the one module both sides already depend on (`core:data` via `api`, `feature:reader` via `implementation`) and it is the pure-Kotlin module with **zero** `android.*` imports, so a `const val String` belongs there. `BorderCropCacheKey.VALUE` is now the single definition; `BorderCropTransformation.CACHE_KEY` delegates to it, and `readerPageMemoryCacheKey` references it directly. **Verified as the property it claims to be, not by inspection:** the gate's own grep over `feature/*/src/main` is now empty, and a repo-wide search for the literal `ephyra-border-crop-v` returns **exactly one hit** — the contract. A second copy cannot hide anywhere. **Falsified**, because "it delegates" is only a claim until something breaks: replacing the delegation with a literal turns the new guard red. `BorderCropTransformationTest` gained `the reported cache key is the domain contract, not a second copy of it`, asserting both `CACHE_KEY` and the instance `cacheKey` equal the contract — so the drift is now caught by a test rather than by a comment, and the probe was reverted. `PageImageCacheKeyTest`'s existing assertion that `CACHE_KEY == BorderCropTransformation().cacheKey` still holds unchanged, because delegation makes them the same value by construction. **Why this is the answer and not a dodge:** it does not weaken the gate (the grep still runs and now passes on merit), does not duplicate the value (one literal, proven), and does not move the transformation out of `core:data` (it stays where the decode happens). The `presentation-core` → `core:data` export recorded in `B-006` is untouched and remains separate debt. Repo-wide: `:app:assembleDebug`, `testDebugUnitTest` and `spotlessCheck` green, `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **285 suites / 1952 tests / 0 failures**. |
| B-060 | **`build.yml` is red for a reason that is a deliberate architectural choice, not a slip — and the two sides of it were written by different people and never reconciled.** `Quality Checks` fails at *Architecture fitness – no `ephyra.data.*` imports in feature production sources*, on exactly one line: `feature/reader/src/main/kotlin/ephyra/feature/reader/viewer/PageImageCacheKey.kt:3`. **Pre-existing and not caused by the dependency/test work:** the file's blob is `ca59a1f5` at both `90e69ca66` and `HEAD`, nothing in `90e69ca66..HEAD` touches it, and `build.yml` is unchanged across that range. It became visible only because the earlier pushes never ran a green `build.yml` — every `Build & Test App` check in that period reported `skipped`, so the workflow's *other* job was the only thing reporting. **Why the violation exists on purpose.** The gate says a feature must reach data through a contract. Commit `90e69ca66` needed `BorderCropTransformation.CACHE_KEY` in `feature:reader` so the reader's Coil cache key would carry the crop *algorithm version*, and its own docstring records the trade-off: *"the alternative was a second, hand-kept version string that could drift from the one Coil actually uses, which is the defect being fixed."* That is this programme's most repeated root cause — two copies of a rule that must agree, in two modules, with no compiler enforcing it (`B-021`, `DEF-021`, and `B-056`'s stray-`}`) — so duplicating the constant was the worse mistake and was correctly refused. **Why it was not simply fixed here.** Both available resolutions are decisions with an owner, and the repo says so itself: the gate's own comment defers the question to `DATA-001I` (*"whether they should is a data-ownership decision belonging to the `DATA-001I` cutover gate, not to a grep. See `B-027`"*), and `B-006` already records `presentation-core` exporting `core:data` as declared debt. Resolving it properly means either hoisting `CACHE_KEY` (or the whole transformation) into a contract module that both `core:data` and `feature:reader` may depend on, or accepting a second copy of the string. The first is module-boundary work on a path the ledger schedules as a cutover; the second reintroduces the drift class the fix was written to remove. **Neither is a mechanical edit, so neither was made unilaterally.** What is recorded instead is the conflict itself, so the next person does not rediscover it from a red badge. **Also worth stating plainly:** the local verification that passed everything — `assembleDebug`, `testDebugUnitTest` (1951 tests), `spotlessCheck` — cannot see this failure, because the gate is a `grep` and `grep` is not a Gradle task. A local green is not evidence about a `build.yml` step, which is a gap this programme has now hit twice (`B-030`'s "green build that ran nothing", and this). |
| B-059 | **A test that had never run was a flake, and its negative cases were passing vacuously â€” both only became visible once the module could compile.** `c3561f81a`. Repairing `feature:reader`'s test source set (`B-058`) turned `ReaderReadCompletionWiringTest` on for the first time, and `a merge that absorbed the tail completes on the surviving page` then failed inside a full multi-module run â€” `UpdateChapter(#340).await(...) was not called` â€” while passing in isolation and on a forced module rerun. **Not a regression, and established rather than assumed:** the file is byte-identical at `c785bc7a1`, where the module could not compile at all, so the test had never executed. A worktree at that commit produces `BUILD FAILED` with **zero** `feature:reader` result files, against 1,951 tests on `main` now. **Cause:** `checkChapterCompletion` returns the `Job` from `viewModelScope.launchNonCancellable`, and all six call sites discarded it and immediately `coVerify`'d. `UnconfinedTestDispatcher` usually runs the write eagerly, so the omission was invisible and surfaced only when ordering differed â€” a fragility this file's own `setUp` already documents ("verified: the class passes in isolation but failed inside a multi-module run where ordering differed"). **The two `exactly = 0` cases are the more serious half,** because verifying before the coroutine ran means they could not fail: `arriving at the last page backward does not mark the chapter read` and `an unresolved chapter is not completed` were green without proving their subject. All six now `join()`. **Falsified** by forcing `movingForward = true` in `ReaderViewModel`, which turns the backward-navigation test red; the probe was reverted and `ReaderViewModel.kt` is byte-identical to `main`, so the negative tests are load-bearing rather than vacuous. **The general lesson worth keeping:** a suite that cannot compile is not a suite that reports red, it is a suite that reports *nothing* â€” so "green" in this repo has repeatedly meant "did not run", which is the same failure mode as `B-030` and `B-044`, and is why every count here is read from the JUnit XML rather than the exit code. |
| B-058 | **The last three broken test source sets are repaired, and two of the four causes were defects that had been silently disabling the tests' own safety properties rather than merely failing to compile.** `6bd4fd3a1`, following the `B-056` fix for the fourth. **Cause 1 â€” JUnit 5 argument order against a JUnit 4 import.** `CoverCacheTest.kt:122` and `PageByteStoreOwnerTest.kt:113` called `assertEquals(expected, actual, message)`, which is the JUnit 5 `Assertions` form, while importing `org.junit.Assert`. JUnit 4 has no `assertEquals(int, int, String)` â€” its three-argument overloads all take the message *first* â€” so the call matched no overload and Kotlin reported `NONE_APPLICABLE` against a list of near-misses. Reordered to message-first, which is what the rest of both files already does (`PageByteStoreOwnerTest.kt:136`). **Cause 2 â€” `it` inside a receiver lambda.** `BoundedPageByteStoreTest.kt:357` and `:385` were `Thread { ... }.apply { startWorkersOn(it) }`; `apply`'s block has a receiver and no parameter, so `it` does not exist. Worth recording beyond the compile error: `startWorkersOn` is documented as setting the daemon flag *"so a failing assertion cannot wedge the test JVM"*, so this was a safety property that had been quietly off in both concurrency tests. **Cause 3 â€” an assertion that could never hold.** `OkHttpAwaitFailureTest` asserted `assertSame(original, thrown)` and failed on both cases. The assertion was wrong, not the code: `await()` does return OkHttp's own instance, because `withCallSite` mutates `stackTrace` in place and returns `this`. What breaks identity is `runBlocking` â€” resuming through `suspendCancellableCoroutine` lets kotlinx-coroutines' stack-trace recovery substitute a copy of the *same type* with the original chained as its `cause`. **Probed, not assumed:** resuming the same `Continuation` directly with no coroutine boundary gives `same=true`; through `runBlocking` gives `same=false`, class unchanged, `cause=java.io.IOException`. **The replacement assertion is stronger, not weaker** â€” the `DEF-023` defect produced an `IOException` *wrapping* an `UnknownHostException`, so the type-preserving `assertInstanceOf` a reader would reach for would have passed while the defect was live. Asserting the exact class fails for that shape. Falsified by reintroducing the wrapper into `withCallSite`, which turned the resolver test red and was reverted; the plain-IO test correctly stayed green, since an `IOException` wrapping an `IOException` *is* the same type, which is why it asserts the class rather than a subtype. **Result:** `testDebugUnitTest` and `spotlessCheck` green repo-wide; **285 suites / 1951 tests / 0 failures**, against 589 before, because four modules that could not compile now contribute roughly 1,360 unreachable tests. No production code changed â€” the diff is four test files, and `OkHttpExtensions.kt` is byte-identical to `main` after reverting the probe. |
| B-056 | **`ImageUrlPolicyTest` did not compile, so 25 tests of `DEF-023`'s pre-flight check had never run â€” and `main` cannot currently compile four test source sets.** Found 2026-09-29 while verifying a dependency-bump test build, where `spotlessCheck` failed on a trailing-newline violation; `spotlessApply` then failed with `ImageUrlPolicyTest.kt:L187 ktlint Expecting a top level declaration`, which is not a formatting error. `:core:common:compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` gave the real pair: `L142 Only safe (?.) or non-null asserted (!!.) calls are allowed on a nullable receiver of type 'String?'` and `L187 Syntax error: Expecting a top level declaration`. Cause: a stray `}` at L117 closed the class early, stranding five test functions as top-level declarations and leaving the file's final `}` matching nothing. Both files were added by `c72f3812c`, the commit that introduced `ImageUrlPolicy`. Fixed (commit `00efab245`) by removing the premature brace and discharging the null with `requireNotNull`, per the convention documented at `WorkflowRedundancyTest.kt:65`; the 25 tests now run and pass, confirmed from the JUnit XML rather than the exit code, because this programme has a documented precedent of a green build that ran nothing (`B-030`). **Falsified before fixing, because the bumps were the obvious suspect and were not the cause:** a worktree at `origin/main` on spotless 8.10.2 and Gradle 9.7.1 reproduced the failure byte-for-byte, and each file's blob hash is identical to `main`'s. The same run shows `main` fails `compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` in `core:data`, `core:domain`, and `feature:reader` as well (`CoverCacheTest.kt:122`, `BoundedPageByteStoreTest.kt:357`, `PageByteStoreOwnerTest.kt:113`), so **`origin/main` is red on its test source sets and no dependabot PR in this batch was ever verified** â€” all five carried `Build & Test App: skipped`. **Those three are left unfixed here: repairing a nullable-receiver assertion in a byte-budget store is a behavioural judgement, not a bump. SUPERSEDED 2026-09-29 by `B-058`, which repairs all three plus the `OkHttpAwaitFailureTest` failures â€” the concerns recorded here about judging a byte-budget store turned out not to apply, because all three were mechanical (a JUnit 4/5 argument-order mismatch and an `it` inside a receiver lambda) rather than behavioural.** **Also found, and not fixed:** `OkHttpAwaitFailureTest` (2 tests) fails on `assertSame(original, thrown)` even with compilation repaired. `await()` does rethrow the same instance â€” `withCallSite` mutates and returns `this` â€” so the failure is kotlinx-coroutines stack-trace recovery handing back a recovered copy across the `runBlocking` boundary. It asserts identity, which coroutine recovery makes unachievable, so the assertion is wrong rather than the production code; recorded rather than silently weakened, because a test whose stated purpose is "the exception type is the whole message" should be changed deliberately. |
| B-054 | **`PageViewportPinPolicy`'s first falsification was green, and the test was the reason.** Disabling the pin call left all 10 tests passing. Cause: the setup assertion used `store.get(id(0))`, and `get` re-inserts the key to mark it most-recently-used â€” so the page under test became the *last* entry evicted and the test was asserting LRU ordering rather than the pin. `PageByteStore.contains` exists precisely to avoid this ("a presence check that reorders the LRU would let a diagnostic change what gets evicted next"), and the store's own docstring predicted the hazard. Switched to `contains` and re-falsified: disabling the pin now turns exactly the eviction test red. **This is the sixth blind gate in this programme and the second one written by me in the same session** â€” the discipline is necessary and it is not sufficient, because a plausible test that exercises a neighbouring mechanism is indistinguishable from a real one until you remove the mechanism and watch. |
| B-053 | **`PageByteBudget`: the first test written for it found a design flaw in it.** A 48 MiB floor is a sensible *cache* size, but on a 64 MB heap it is **three quarters of the entire per-app budget** â€” the store would not sit beside the renderer, it would evict it. The floor now yields to a `MAX_HEAP_SHARE` ceiling of 1/4 of `ActivityManager.getMemoryClass()`, and the floor is itself clamped by that ceiling so it can never be unachievable. The failure was only visible because the test asserted the *invariant* (the store must never claim a quarter of the heap) rather than restating the constants; a test that had asserted "clamps up to 48 MiB" would have passed against a policy that starves the renderer on small devices. Falsified: removing the heap-share cap turns exactly the two tests that encode the invariant red and leaves the seven number-restating ones green â€” the split is the point. |

| B-029 | **Found 2026-09-26: the JXL container signature check never matched anything.** `isJxl()` compared byte 4 against `0x4A` (`'J'`), but the ISOBMFF box type for JXL is `jXL ` â€” a lowercase `j`, `0x6A`. Every container-form JXL therefore fell through to "not JXL", `Decoder.Factory.create` returned null, and the image silently failed to decode. Only bare codestreams beginning `FF 0A` ever worked, so the bug was invisible for any input that happened to use that form. Caught by `JxlSignatureTest`, which asserts the container **positively**; a test that only asserted "JXL is recognised, other formats are not" would have passed had the whole method returned `true` unconditionally for images, and would not have localised this. Fixed to `0x6A`, falsification-verified both directions. **Second, separate finding: the task's own instruction is currently unexecutable.** The row says "the fixture binary is immutable: hash-verify before use", but **no `.jxl` file exists anywhere in the tree** â€” a full filesystem walk returns only the decoder source. There is nothing to hash-verify, so `TST-001C3`'s decode leg cannot be attempted, let alone completed, and the row is `BLOCKED` rather than `IN_PROGRESS`. A fixture must be sourced and independently reviewed before that leg can start. | OPEN â€” bug fixed and guarded; fixture acquisition still outstanding |
| B-028 | **RESOLVED as a decision, 2026-09-28 â€” owner ruled; see ADR-0011.** (1) ~~I recommended `NAV-001` while it is forbidden by a non-negotiable rule.~~ Withdrawn; the sequencing constraint no longer applies now that Phase 2 and Phase 7 are `VERIFIED`. (2) **The "confirmed double `NavHost`" claim was not investigated and does not hold.** `MainActivity.kt:251` owns app-level destinations and `HomeScreen.kt:178` owns the five bottom tabs; **nested `NavHost`s for bottom tabs is the standard Android pattern**, because each tab needs its own back stack. Flattening them is a design decision with real costs, not a cleanup. Relatedly, `NAV-001` asked to "delete duplicate string route hierarchies" but there is exactly **one** `ScreenRoutes.kt`, in `presentation-core` â€” nothing to delete. **Owner decision, 2026-09-28: keep the nested tab `NavHost`.** [ADR-0011](adr/0011-one-graph-coordinator-not-one-navhost.md) amends ADR-0002 to require one *coordinator* and typed routes rather than one physical `NavHost`, and identifies the defect ADR-0002 was actually reaching for: the global `MutableStateFlow`/`MutableSharedFlow` navigation events in `presentation-core/.../ui/navigation/ScreenRoutes.kt`. The features already register through a `FeatureApi` contract, which is a coordinator rather than sibling callbacks. `NAV-001` is re-scoped accordingly. Separately, the `SEC-002` audit found six trackers persisting OAuth bearer tokens in plaintext; **that is now resolved** â€” see the `SEC-002` row, which reached `CODE_COMPLETE at E3` with a Keystore-backed cipher and no new dependency edge (`android.security.keystore` is platform API from API 23). | **CLOSED** â€” owner decision recorded in ADR-0011; `SEC-002` item resolved |
| B-027 | **Found 2026-09-26: `origin/main` was red, and it was a gate that should never have existed in that form.** `Build & Test` [#1216](https://github.com/Gameaday/Ephyra/actions/runs/36245887484) failed on `ephyra.data.* import count (1) exceeds baseline (0)`, which cascaded to a skipped `build` job and a failed nightly. **The failure predates this session** â€” the offending import was introduced by `d1bf86a11` (2026-09-25) in `feature/reader/src/test/.../SyntheticMediaFixturesTest.kt`, verified as an ancestor of `origin/main`. Two separate defects: (1) the step was a **count baseline**, satisfiable by editing `BASELINE` in the workflow, which is the metric-as-measure antipattern `BUILD_HEALTH.md` exists to prevent; (2) it grepped `feature/*/src`, so it failed on a **test** file, which is not a layering violation. Replaced with a structural rule scoped to `feature/*/src/main`, and **falsification-verified**: a planted production import makes it fail, the real tree passes, and the pre-fix count gate is confirmed to still fail on the test file. **The underlying question is deliberately left open, not hidden:** `feature/reader` declares `implementation(projects.core.data)`, so whether a feature may depend on data at all is a data-ownership decision belonging to the `DATA-001I` cutover gate, not to a grep. | B-026 | **Correction, 2026-09-26: the two behaviour changes I gave as reasons to separate `RDR-004` stage 2 were both wrong.** I claimed the gesture swap would "defer the first tap by the double-tap interval, a 350ms delay on every page turn" and would "drop the long-press suppression while zoomed." Reading the pager's own handler in `ZoomableMangaPage` shows the legacy code **already** defers at-fit single taps with `delay(350L)` (line 242) and **already** guards long-press with `!transformStarted && !wasMultiTouch` (line 449). | B-034 | **Re-audit of the 25-contract list by behaviour rather than by type name, 2026-09-26.** `B-033` showed the name-based check produces false positives, so the list was re-tested by asking whether the *behaviour* each contract defines exists inline in shipping code. **Result: the list splits into two groups, and the distinction matters more than the count.** (a) **Duplicated behaviour** â€” the shipping app already implements the logic, so the contract is a specification that nothing is forced to obey, and the unwired state is arguably fine until the replacement reader lands: `MissingChapters` (missing-chapter logic in `ExtensionLoader`, `MangaScreen`), `CanonicalDeduplicator` (dedup in `ExtensionManager`, `ExtensionLoader`), `MediaMatcher`/`ContentMappers`/`ContentMappersReverse` (ingest mapping in `ContentRepositoryImpl`, `MangaRestorer`, `BrowseSourceViewModel`), `AnimationPolicy` (7 files animate directly), `LibraryFlags` and `RenderPathPolicy` (flag and crop/slice decisions in `ExtensionApi` and `ComposeWebtoonReader`). (b) **Genuinely absent behaviour** â€” the shipping app has no equivalent, so the contract describes something not yet built: the `PageImage`/`PageMetadata`/`PageSource`/`PageSourceId`/`DecodePlan`/`BoundedPageByteStore`/`DocumentViewport`/`DocumentTilePartition`/`TileScalePolicy`/`WebtoonViewport` family (30 files still use `ReaderPage` directly), plus `WorkflowContract`, `SeriesPersistenceContract`, `DownloadArtifactIndexContract`, `ReconcileSourceRegistry`, `LegacySeriesMigration`, `IngestEngine`. **The honest conclusion is that "unwired" was never one condition.** Group (a) needs no urgent work; group (b) is the real 2.0 surface and is blocked on the phase gate, not on effort. I have stopped counting, because a single number here would misrepresent both groups. **CORRECTED 2026-09-28 â€” four claims above are wrong, and two are wrong in the direction that would have caused real damage.** Re-verified behaviourally, not by name. (1) **`LibraryFlags` is not a type.** `LibraryFlagsTest.kt` exercises `LibrarySort` and `LibraryDisplayMode`, and both are **heavily wired** â€” `LibraryViewModel.kt:402-453,768`, `LibrarySettingsDialog.kt:198-276`, `LibraryPager.kt:68-112`, `feature/browse`'s `BrowseSourceScreen.kt`/`BrowseSourceToolbar.kt`. The row listed an unwired contract that does not exist. (2) **`ContentMappersReverse` is not a type either** â€” it is the reverse-direction half of `ContentMappers.kt`, and it is wired: `ContentRepositoryImpl.kt:37`, `ContentUnitRepositoryImpl.kt:31,36`, `MangaRestorer.kt:119,167`, `BrowseSourceViewModel.kt:304`, `NotificationReceiver.kt:238`. (4) **`StartupReducer` is in the wrong group, and my own correction of it this morning was *also* wrong.** I first moved it from (b) to (a) on the claim that `App.kt` "performs the same three startup steps (`LOAD_PREFERENCES`/`REGISTER_WORKERS`/`RECONCILE_SOURCES`) imperatively." **Checked before migrating it, that claim does not hold.** `App.kt` has **no source reconciliation at all** â€” the only `SourceManager` reference is a cover-fetcher dependency. `StartupTracker.Phase` has 7 entries, not 4, and `StartupGuard` tracks 7 *different* phase names (`logging`, `crash_handler`, `di_container`, `telemetry`, `notifications`, `reactive_bindings`, `async_init`) that do not correspond to `StartupStep` at all. `REGISTER_WORKERS` corresponds to one line (`workManagerConfiguration`). **`RECONCILE_SOURCES` has no implementation to migrate onto, so wiring the reducer would mean *adding* a startup step, not adopting one.** **The correction that matters most is the method, not the rows:** two entries were not types at all, one was a 3-to-7 mismatch asserted without reading the file, and all three were reached by trusting a name or a summary. `B-033` had already established that name-based checks give false positives; this shows the same failure reaching a *classification* and then surviving a correction. **Any future pass over this list must open the file before classifying it.** | AUDITED â€” 4 rows corrected, 1 correction itself corrected; count framing retired in favour of the `B-032` wiring sequence. **Update 2026-11-06 (A3):** the `RECONCILE_SOURCES` gap named here now has an implementation — `SourceReconciler.kt`, on the shipping source authority; see the `ARC-003` row. |

| B-025 | **Found 2026-09-26 while attempting the stage-1 cutover that `RDR-004` had just completed. The shipping continuous reader uses `WebtoonDocumentZoom` (`scale` plus `offsetX` only) and returns a `scrollCorrection`, because the `LazyColumn` owns vertical position. `DocumentViewport` carries `scale`, `offset.x` and `offset.y`, clamps both axes, and contains no `scroll` or `correction` concept at all. Wiring the target model in would give the continuous surface **two owners of vertical position**; they would fight, and it would surface as content that jumps on the exact surface where `DEF-002` and `DEF-003` are meant to improve. The paged reader had no such conflict because one page means one transform and one owner. **Decision:** `WebtoonDocumentZoom` remains the correct model for the `LazyColumn`-backed surface, and `DocumentViewport` is the model for a future canvas-backed surface. (1) Injecting the `E4LabFixtureReceiver` into `app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` turned `the release manifest does not register an E4-lab receiver` red. (2) Planting `E4LabProbe.kt` under `app/src/main` turned `no E4-lab scaffolding exists in a shippable source set` red, naming the file. **The limit found on the way:** the rule reads `git ls-files`, so it only sees *tracked* files. The probe passed while untracked and failed once staged. CI is covered -- everything is committed there -- but a new leak is caught in CI rather than by the local pre-commit hook, which is a real gap in the fast loop and is recorded rather than glossed. **And the same stale-task trap for the third time in this programme:** the first re-run reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1s` with the test task `UP-TO-DATE`, so `failures=0` was meaningless. The pattern is identical to `B-041` and the baseline-profile gate: a repository-reading test whose inputs Gradle does not track, so it silently does not re-run. Three occurrences now justify treating it as a standing hazard rather than a one-off. | RESOLVED -- both rules verified; the untracked-file window is a known gap |
| B-062 | **The library sort control did nothing, and the cause was a value read outside the flow it feeds.** Reported as "sort does not work (at least on the library screen)". The re-sort pipeline was keyed on `LibraryData` equality, but `applySort` read the sort from `Category.flags` and `randomSortSeed().getSync()` - both outside the flow. Changing the sort therefore produced no new `LibraryData`, `distinctUntilChanged()` discarded the emission, and the grid kept its old order until the library *contents* changed. The Default tab made it look permanently dead: it is synthesized with `flags = 0` and has no database row, so `LibrarySort.valueOf` always decoded it as Alphabetical/Descending. Two consequences beyond the reported one: the sort dialog highlighted the wrong option on that same tab (it read `category.sort`), and re-tapping Random wrote a seed nothing observed. Fixed by carrying the observed sort mode, seed and categorized-display flag in `LibraryData`, giving one owner (`sortModeFor`) of "whose sort wins" that both the grid and the dialog resolve through, and making `SetSortModeForCategory` base new flags on the persisted mode instead of rebuilding from `0` when the category lookup misses. Also replaced an `error(...)` in the Random comparator that was one refactor away from being a live crash. | `feature/library/src/main/kotlin/ephyra/feature/library/LibraryViewModel.kt`, `core/domain/src/main/java/ephyra/domain/category/interactor/SetSortModeForCategory.kt`, `feature/library/src/test/kotlin/ephyra/feature/library/LibraryViewModelTest.kt` | **RESOLVED 2026-10-04** - three regression tests, each falsification-verified by reintroducing the defect and watching the test fail before restoring the fix, per the rule in `doc/BUILD_HEALTH.md` that a green run proves nothing until the gate is watched to fail. |
| B-063 | **`spotlessCheck` was already red on `main`, and it masked the entire unit-test job.** Found 2026-10-04 by reading the run list rather than by watching a local run, because the gate had been failing since `4c3208ae1`. Format violations in `LibraryContent.kt` and `SettingsTrackingScreen.kt`, plus a missing final newline in `MotionConsistencyTest.kt` - none of them in code that run had touched. The important part is the blast radius: `Build & Test App` `needs: quality`, so a formatting failure in the lint job skipped the job that runs `testDebugUnitTest` outright. Two genuinely failing tests were sitting behind it and no run had reported them. Read from the API for run `579c7ba32`: `Quality Checks` failed at `Check code format` and `Build & Test App` was `skipped`. | `.github/workflows/build.yml`, `feature/library/src/main/kotlin/ephyra/feature/library/presentation/components/LibraryContent.kt`, `app/src/test/java/ephyra/app/architecture/MotionConsistencyTest.kt` | **RESOLVED 2026-10-04** - via `spotlessApply`; import order and blank lines only. |
| B-064 | **Two unit tests were failing on `main`, hidden behind `B-063`, and one of them was the dispatcher gate doing its job.** Found 2026-10-04 by running the suite locally, which is the only way to see them while the lint job short-circuits the test job. (1) `CategoryViewModelTest > change order calls interactor` raced the 500ms `REORDER_DEBOUNCE_MILLIS` that `83f47bc39` introduced: `changeOrder` debounces the write, so verifying without advancing the virtual clock asserted against a coroutine that had not run. It now advances the clock. (2) `InjectableViewModelDispatcherTest > a ViewModel does not launch on a dispatcher no test can control` failed because `SettingsTrackingViewModel` (from `39316f01e`) hardcoded `Dispatchers.IO` - precisely the shape the gate exists to prevent, and the same shape that already cost one CI failure per that test's own docstring. Fixed by injecting `@IoDispatcher` following the pattern the gate names, rather than appending to the allowlist, which the test documents as "debt, not permission". Worth noting the sequence: both regressions shipped while the lint gate was red, so neither was visible in any run. | `feature/category/src/test/kotlin/ephyra/feature/category/CategoryViewModelTest.kt`, `feature/settings/src/main/kotlin/ephyra/feature/settings/screen/SettingsTrackingViewModel.kt`, `app/src/test/java/ephyra/app/architecture/InjectableViewModelDispatcherTest.kt` | **RESOLVED 2026-10-04** - verified green together with the whole suite (2169 tests, 0 failures) on one uncontended run. |

The transform centroid was also discarded, so zoom was not anchored to the fingers. | `PagerZoomPolicy` 16 tests (focal invariant, pan clamping, double-tap) + E4 matrix | **DEVICE_VERIFIED.** `E3` is `PagerZoomPolicy` (16 tests) plus `PagerViewportRenderTest` and `PagerGestureInjectTest`; `E4-lab` is `doc/evidence/README.md#e4lab-def-001-pinch-transform`, a real two-finger pinch on API 37 recording `scale 1.0 -> 5.0, raster 1080x2424 -> 5400x12120` (exactly 5x) through 28 arbiter effects. **Falsified at the defect seam:** an identity `pagerZoomLayer` leaves the scale moving while the raster is unchanged, and the assertion names that as the DEF-001 defect. Production-wired: `pagerZoomLayer(state.transform)` is the single seam. |
| DEF-002 | Webtoon pinch mainly widened content: the per-item transform hardcoded `scaleY = 1f`, and the gesture centroid was never passed to the zoom state, so the strip was not anchored to the fingers either. | `WebtoonZoomPolicy` 22 tests (focal invariant, pan clamp, coverage, scroll symmetry) + E4 matrix | **CODE_COMPLETE at E3, PRODUCTION-WIRED.** The device leg is now proven on `emulator-5554` (API 37) by `WebtoonZoomRenderTest`, which captures the real raster of the scroll container and asserts the document transform scales it. `DEF-002` is falsification-verified in the strongest form available: restoring the original `scaleY = 1f` turns the test red with the measured failure `height went 2424 -> 2424`, so the assertion is a test of the reported defect rather than of scaling in general. Only `E4-user` remains, which is deferred by decision. **A4 update (2026-11-07):** the transform this row certifies is no longer wired inline in `ComposeWebtoonReader` — it is extracted verbatim into `webtoonDocumentTransform` + `WebtoonZoomGestureHost`, so the captured `E3` raster evidence remains valid and the whole pointer path is re-drivable at `E2` on every CI run. The extraction also closed a defect the `E3` raster could not see: `GestureCancelled` was a production no-op (`committed = null`); a cancelled gesture now restores the committed transform, pinned by test. |
| DEF-003 | Sliced webtoon elements overlapped because zoom was applied per LazyColumn item: a scaled item paints outside its layout slot while the list retains the unscaled geometry, so neighbours collide. The transform now lives once on the scroll container, so no per-item painted bounds can disagree with layout. |`WebtoonZoomPolicy` coverage tests + E4 matrix| **CODE_COMPLETE at E3** (2026-09-27). `WebtoonZoomRenderTest.sliceBoundariesMoveWithTheDocumentTransform` measures *where slice boundaries land*, not merely that the image changed. The measurement is a fraction of raster height rather than raw pixels, because `captureToImage` returns a bitmap sized to the node's rendered bounds: comparing raw pixel offsets across two bitmaps of different sizes reports a 4x ratio for a 2x transform, which is a false `DEF-003` â€” I wrote that version first and it failed. **Falsified at the real defect:** moving the transform from the container onto each item, the original arrangement, turns this test red while the other five in the class stay green. The measured signature is exact: the boundary sits at 0.2166 of the raster at fit *and* at 2x, where a correct container transform puts it at 0.4332. A per-item transform scales the paint but not the slot, so the boundary is stationary relative to what the user sees. |
