| DEF-005 | Series-to-Library return motion: the shared-element container enter/exit used mismatched durations (300ms in, 200ms out), so the two screens crossed at different points while the cover competed with a still-fading container. Easing was also opposed (decelerate in, accelerate out) over the same period. | `MotionPolicyTest` (17 tests, green) plus a **wiring check on 2026-09-26 that corrects an earlier error in this row** | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2, PRODUCTION-WIRED â€” verified wired 2026-09-26.** I first downgraded this row claiming `MotionPolicy` was unwired. That was wrong: I searched for the class name and missed the actual path. `MotionPolicy.SHARED_ELEMENT_DURATION_MILLIS` is read by `Motion.sharedElementContainerDuration()`, which is what **both** `m3SharedElementContainerEnter` and `m3SharedElementContainerExit` pass to their `tween`, and `MainActivity` calls all four with a live `rememberSystemReducedMotion()`. The duration is symmetric, the easing is `LinearEasing` on both sides rather than opposed, and reduced motion is honoured. The defect as reported is fixed in the shipping app. Only `E3`/`E4-user` remain. Method lesson in `B-033`: verifying wiring by grepping for a type name is not verification. |
| DEF-006 | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2**, production-wired. | **No test.** A sweep of every test source set for an updates/library scope policy returns nothing, so the rule is unenforced by anything. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-27 â€” the guarantee now exists.** Re-verified with the corrected method: `GetUpdates.subscribe` took `libraryOnly: Boolean = false` while `UpdatesViewModel` passed `libraryOnly = true` at its only call site, so the shipping app was already correct and the reported symptom was absent. The defect was the *guarantee*: the permissive value was the default, so a future consumer omitting the argument would silently receive the whole catalogue â€” the reported defect one call site away, and invisible in review. **Fix:** the default is now `true` on both `GetUpdates.subscribe` and `UpdatesRepository.subscribeAll`. Opting out is now the explicit, deliberate act. **Behaviour-preserving today**, which is what made it safe to do without a device: there is exactly one caller of the scoped overload and it already passed `true`; the widgets use the separate two-argument `subscribe(read, after)` and are unaffected. `GetUpdatesScopeTest` (2) pins the default rather than the current caller, so a regression is a failing test instead of a silent behaviour change. **Falsified:** reverting the default to `false` turned exactly the guarantee test red and left the opt-out test green. | Read state and update scoping are user-visible, so the guarantee matters more than the current behaviour: a new screen that forgot the argument would show every update in the catalogue and nothing would fail. Verified by falsification, not by inspection. ||
| DEF-007 | Backward chapter navigation must remain available with skip-read enabled. | `ReaderChapterWindowPolicyTest` (13 tests, green, including "skip read applies forward but never hides the previous chapter") | **CODE_COMPLETE — wired 2026-11-06 (A2) via `ReaderChapterWindowAdapter.kt`; the behaviour was already correct.** The shipping path already implements the required behaviour and **three of the policy's four switches inline**: `getChapterList` applies `skipDupe` and `downloadedOnly`, and skip-read is applied forward-only as an `eligible` predicate, so backward navigation is preserved. The fourth switch, `skipFilteredForward`, is deliberately **not** applied â€” the reader's navigation universe is documented as independent of the series screen's view filters, so following "next" should not walk the user off the list they are navigating. That is a defensible position, and it means the unwired policy is not evidence of a missing feature. **The behaviour is covered at the call site**: `ChapterNavigationPolicyTest` (3 tests) pins backward-always-adjacent, forward-skips-read-only-when-enabled, and explicit boundaries. The 13 policy tests describe the richer contract but do not govern shipping behaviour. Wiring it remains a migration of chapter ordering and filtering, not a call-site change. Corrected from a bare `CODE_COMPLETE`, which asserted neither the behaviour nor the wiring. **2026-11-06 (A2): the wiring is done.** `ReaderViewModel` now selects prev/next through `ReaderChapterWindowAdapter.kt` (backed by the domain policy); the series-screen filters stay upstream in `getChapterList` rather than being re-run by the domain policy, and `skipFilteredForward` stays off by the documented decision above. The call-site characterisation moved from the deleted `ChapterNavigationPolicyTest` to `ReaderChapterWindowAdapterTest.kt` (6 tests). No JDK locally; remote CI is the verification channel. |
| DEF-008 | Crop-borders transformation measured insets transposed, aborted on a single artifact, and cropped contentless images. | `BorderCropTransformationTest` (8 Robolectric tests, incl. a real JPEG round-trip) **plus `BorderCropDeviceTest` (4, `E3`)** | **CODE_COMPLETE at E3.** `core:data` now has a working `androidTest` source set with a declared `testInstrumentationRunner` and the API 37 espresso pin, so the decoded pixels can be asserted on the device rather than only under Robolectric. The four device cases cover a uniform border being removed, a full-bleed page left alone, a blank page never cropped, and **asymmetric insets measured per edge**. That last one is the one that matters: a symmetric fixture cannot detect transposition at all, because swapping left/top and right/bottom on a uniform border yields identical numbers. Falsification-verified â€” reintroducing the transposition produces `expected:<360> but was 352`, naming `DEF-008` in the failure. The first version of this suite used a symmetric border and passed *with the bug present*; the falsification probe is what exposed that, which is the second time in this programme a green suite has been shown to be blind. Only `E4-user` remains. |
| DEF-009 | Long-strip reading-mode precedence lived in `ReaderViewModel` with untested content-type handling. | `DefaultReadingModeResolverTest` (8 tests, green) | CODE_COMPLETE at E2, PRODUCTION-WIRED â€” **verified wired 2026-09-26**: `DefaultReadingModeResolver` is consumed by `feature/reader/ReaderViewModel.kt`, so the precedence rule the defect describes is the one the app applies. | DEF-011 | **Merged chapters could be marked fully read after a single page turn, 2026-09-26.** Reported by the owner as: merge used, open, flip a page, back out, and the chapter is already marked read. | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2**, production-wired; device confirmation outstanding. | **Root cause:** `ReaderViewModel` decided chapter completion with `pageIndex == lastIndex \|\| pages.drop(pageIndex+1).all { it.isHidden }`. `isHidden` is `isAbsorbed \|\| isBlockedByFilter`, and `isAbsorbed` is set by merge â€” so a merged chapter has a hidden tail by construction, and passing the first visible page satisfied the rule. Three defects, not one: the rule was **duplicated verbatim** at two call sites and had already drifted in null-handling; `[].all{}` is vacuously `true`, so an out-of-range index completed a chapter on an empty tail; and it never consulted `state`, so it could complete a chapter whose page list was populated but not yet loaded. **Fix:** one pure `ChapterCompletionPolicy` in `core:domain` with 13 tests, shared by both call sites â€” no inline copy remains. **Falsified:** removing the three guards turned exactly the 5 guard tests red and left the 8 rule tests green, so the tests target the defects rather than the whole policy. A second layer, `ReaderReadCompletionWiringTest` (7 tests), now drives the real `ReaderViewModel` and asserts on the `ChapterUpdate` that reaches `UpdateChapter` rather than on the policy's return value, so the *consumption* is covered as well as the rule; it also revealed that the `chapterResolved` argument is unreachable on this path (`ReaderChapter.pages` is derived from `State.Loaded`, so the `?: return null` above is what actually stops completion). | Read state drives tracking (`updateTrackChapterRead`) and duplicate-chapter propagation, so a wrong flag corrupts more than the badge. **Not** confirmed on a device: the reported repro was not reproduced here, so this fix is argued from the code, not from an observed failure. |
| DEF-010 | **Found and FIXED 2026-09-26 on API 37: Discover â†’ Local source showed a perpetual loading spinner and never listed content, whether or not any content existed.** **CODE_COMPLETE at E3**, `DEF-010` fix verified on device and by unit test. | **Root cause:** `LocalSource.ID` is `0L` â€” the only legitimate source id that is not positive. `BrowseSourceViewModel` guarded with `sourceId <= 0L`, conflating that with the `State.sourceId = -1L` "not set" sentinel, and returned `emptyFlow()`. Under `SharingStarted.Lazily` that empty flow is terminal, so the screen never loaded. The same conflation at `navSourceId > 0L` silently skipped restoring a Local source browse after process death. **Fix:** both guards now reject only negative ids. **Verified:** a unit test asserting `GetRemoteManga` is invoked for id `0` went red before the fix (`call 1 of 1 â€¦ was not called`) and green after; on device the Local source now lists `E4-Lab Series` and the `ProgressBar` is gone. Screenshot: `doc/evidence/e4lab-reader-page1.png`. **Fourth blind test this session:** `mangaPagerFlowFlow emits when initialized` asserted `assertNotNull` on the outer `StateFlow`, which is seeded with `emptyFlow()` and always emits â€” it passed even in the broken state. It now asserts the paging source is actually requested, and a companion test pins the negative sentinel as still-rejected. | Only the Local source was affected, which is why it survived: every remote source id is positive. Found *because* of E4-lab seeding, not caused by it. |
| DEF-018 | **A reader page evicted from the disk cache could never load again â€” silently blank, never errored.** Owner-reported 2026-09-28 as "missed images". | `PageRequeueStructuralTest` (4) asserting the eviction branch resets *and* re-offers; `HttpPageLoader.kt` eviction branch | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2, falsification-verified.** `page.status = Page.State.Queue` is a *status*, not an enqueue. Nothing put the page back on the priority queue, the pager's only load trigger is `LaunchedEffect(page)` keyed on page *identity* so it cannot re-fire on a status change, and the worker takes only entries actually queued. The page stayed `Queue` forever. Now re-offers at the dequeued priority. **Not yet observed on a device** â€” reachability is established from the three source conditions, not a reproduced eviction. |
| DEF-019 | **`fetchAndCacheImage` reported success having written nothing.** | `ChapterCache.fetchAndCacheImage` dedup/failure discrimination | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2.** `openEditor()` returning null means either a concurrent write (legitimate dedup) or eviction pressure (nothing written); the second returned success, so the caller marked the page `Ready` and handed out a stream yielding null forever â€” reproducing the blank page deterministically rather than by race. Distinguished by probing for an existing snapshot. |
| DEF-020 | **A `403` on a signed or time-limited image URL was treated as permanent, after a full futile backoff ladder.** | `TransientErrorsTest` (7), incl. the explicit status delta the reader previously mishandled | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2, falsification-verified.** The loader also never re-resolved `imageUrl` on retry, so all three attempts requested the same revoked URL. Now re-resolves on every retry; `403`/`410` transient; `404`/`401`/`400` still permanent so this is not "retry everything". |
| DEF-021 | **The retry classification existed in two copies across modules, which is how `DEF-020` drifted.** | One `core:common` `TransientErrors`; `HttpPageLoader.kt` and `core/download/Downloader.kt` both route through it | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2.** Same shape as the triplicated download path rule fixed earlier in this pass: two copies of a rule that must agree, in two modules, with no compiler enforcing it. |
| DEF-023 | **A page whose image host does not resolve could not be recovered, "even after retry".** Owner-reported 2026-09-29: some pages fail with `Unable to resolve host "<cdn host>": No address associated with hostname`, and pressing Retry repeats the same failure. | `TransientErrorsTest` (the resolution case, incl. surviving re-wrapping and the bounded walk); `OkHttpAwaitFailureTest` (the exception type survives `await`); `PageRetryUrlPolicyStructuralTest` (the loader asks the classifier and the eviction reloads keep their URL); `PageListSavePolicyTest` (a URL dropped this session is persisted, and an unchanged list is not) | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2 for the policy; the wiring is gated structurally. NOW COMPILED AND RUN in CI — `PageListSavePolicyTest` 5, `PageRetryUrlPolicyStructuralTest` 6, `PageRequeueStructuralTest` 4 and `TransientErrorsTest` all green in run `36746376376` (`c7f9a42e`), which this row previously said could not be compiled. NOT OBSERVED ON DEVICE** Three defects in one chain, all read out of the code rather than reproduced: **`Call.await()` re-wrapped every failure as `IOException(e.message, e)`**, keeping the message and the original as `cause` while throwing away the *type* â€” and a resolver failure is the one type that says the URL is at fault rather than the connection, so nothing downstream could act on it. **`TransientErrors.shouldReResolveUrl` knew only 403/410**, so a resolution failure was *retried* (correctly, it is an `IOException`) always against the same URL (incorrectly); it now walks the cause chain for `UnknownHostException` and stale statuses, depth-bounded so the walk terminates. **`HttpPageLoader` decided the question with `retries > 0`**, which answers it wrongly at both ends: it re-resolves after a `429` where the same URL is correct, and it does *not* re-resolve on the first attempt of a user Retry, because a fresh `internalLoadPage` starts the counter at zero and the reload kept the failed URL â€” so the button re-sent the request that had just failed. The loader now asks the shared classifier, at the point of failure (the page stops holding an indicted URL) and in `prepareForReload` for a user-triggered reload. **The counterweight is asserted too:** the cache-eviction reloads still keep their URL, where nothing indicted it. **`recycle` had a matching hole and is fixed with it:** the save decision was `cacheHadMissingImageUrls` alone, which describes the list as it was *loaded* and cannot see a URL dropped mid-session, so a dead URL stayed in the persisted page list and every later open of that chapter began by spending a request on a host already known to be dead; it is now `needsPageListSave`, a pure function of the flag and the URLs the pages hold now. **Tried and reverted, recorded because a reverted change is still information:** routing the page error through `formattedMessage` looked like an easy win on a raw resolver string and is not â€” with connectivity present, the reported case renders as `Couldn't reach Unable to resolve host "â€¦": No address associated with hostname`, which is *worse* than what it replaced, and it prefixed two unrelated `IllegalStateException("Page image is unavailable")` call sites with the class name. The error copy is not this defect. **Caveats, stated because this programme's own rule is that an unverified claim is not a verified one:** (a) nothing in this change has been compiled or executed â€” the working environment has no JDK, no Gradle cache and no Android SDK â€” so the four test files are written to be run, not run, and the only evidence here is that the two policy objects are asserted by *running them* while the loader's wiring is checked structurally for the same reason `PageRequeueStructuralTest` gives; (b) the DNS failure has **not** been reproduced on a device, so reachability is established from the code, not observed; (c) a fresh URL is *likely* but not *guaranteed* to differ: a source whose image URLs are minted per request will usually hand back a different host, which is the only thing that can recover from a dead one, and that is a property of the source rather than of this fix. **Deliberately not changed:** the backoff schedule. A longer ladder would park a worker for longer, and on a LOW-tier device there is one worker, so lengthening it trades the page the user is looking at for the page that is already failing â€” a tuning decision that needs the measurement `P0-3` is already deferred pending, not a guess made in a bug fix. Also not changed: the downloader, which reuses `isTransient` and whose URLs come from the chapter's cached page list, where re-resolving means a page-list refetch and is a separate change with its own cost. |
| DEF-012 | **Trigger corrected 2026-09-27: not backup-specific â€” see `DEF-017`.** **A `Float` round trip silently broke every chapter-number comparison that crossed the database boundary, so read state stopped propagating for decimal chapter numbers.** Found 2026-09-26 while auditing the read/tracking path the owner flagged after `DEF-011`. | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2**, production-wired. | **Root cause:** `Chapter.chapterNumber` is a `Double`, but it arrives by two routes that disagree. `ChapterRecognition.parseChapterNumber` reads "12.3" and returns the nearest `Double`; the value is then **narrowed to `Float` on the way into the database** and widened back on the way out â€” `12.3` becomes `12.300000190734863` and stays that way. `Double.equals` is exact bit comparison, so a freshly parsed chapter and the same chapter read back from the database are not equal. A backup `Float` field is one more place the same narrowing happens, not the trigger; see `DEF-017`. Two call sites used `==` across that boundary. **Failure shape:** data-dependent and silent â€” appears only after a database round trip, only for numbers with no exact `Float` representation, and never throws. **Fix:** one pure `ChapterNumber` policy in `core:domain` â€” `sameChapterNumber` within a magnitude-aware tolerance, used by both call sites â€” which also refuses to call unrecognised numbers equal, since that would collapse a series whose source reports no numbers. **Extended to ordering:** the same root cause reaches tracking through `<=`, and fails in the opposite direction. `ChapterNumber.hasReached` covers that, biased toward counting at the boundary â€” the safe direction for read state. A follow-up sweep found two more sites, the worst being `ShouldUpdateDbChapter`, which asks `dbChapter.chapterNumber != sourceChapter.chapterNumber` and therefore **rewrites every chapter on every sync, forever**, for a difference that does not exist. `GenerateAuthorityChapters` was checked and deliberately left alone: its `lastChapterRead` parameter is an `Int`, so no narrowing is possible on either side. **Falsified twice** by weakening the policy. | Not observed on a device â€” reachability is established from the declared types and the arithmetic, not from a reproduced restore. |
| DEF-027 | **A page whose URL the source named relatively could not be requested at all, and the reader showed OkHttp's internal message.** Owner-reported 2026-09-30: viewing a manga page failed with `Expected URL scheme 'http' or 'https' but no scheme was found for …`; the page was present at the source site and was not rate limited. | `ImageUrlPolicyResolveTest` (13 methods / 38 cases) for the rule; `HttpSourceImageUrlRequestTest` builds the real `Request` from a relative `Page.url`; `HttpPageLoaderUrlResolutionTest` asserts the address reaching `ChapterCache.fetchAndCacheImage` | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2, falsification-verified. NOT OBSERVED ON DEVICE.** **Root cause, and why the report's own facts are the clue rather than a contradiction:** that exception is raised by `toHttpUrl()` while a `Request` is *built*. Nothing was sent, so no host was contacted, no rate limit applied, and the page being live at the source is not in tension with it — the address simply could not become a request. Nothing absolutised a relative page URL anywhere in the tree. `HttpPageLoader.getPages` copies `Page.url` verbatim; upstream resolved such URLs in `HttpSource.getImageUrl`, but this fork's `getImageUrl` is the deprecated *network* variant (`fetchImageUrl` → `imageUrlRequest` → `GET(page.url)`), so the resolution existed nowhere. **`DEF-023`'s guard could not catch it, and the reason is structural:** `ImageUrlPolicy.requireUsable` runs *after* `getImageUrl` returns and inspects `imageUrl`, never `Page.url`. As an `IllegalArgumentException` the failure was classified **permanent** — no backoff ladder — and `shouldReResolveUrl` false, so the user's own Retry re-queued the identical inputs and repeated it verbatim; `ReaderPageErrorView` prints `error.message`, which is how OkHttp's wording reached the screen at all. **Fix: resolve rather than reject.** A relative URL is incomplete, not broken, and the missing half is a value the app already holds — so no new throw site was added and `requireUsable` remains the backstop for what genuinely cannot address a host. `ImageUrlPolicy.resolve` is the single owner (ADR-0001) and is applied at every boundary that can reach a request, since any one of them can be the last: `HttpSource.imageUrlRequest` and `imageRequest`; the loader, which additionally covers a URL restored from the chapter cache or set directly by `pageListParse`; and `Downloader`, which had **no** `ImageUrlPolicy` call at all and would have stored the same bytes twice under two spellings. `DynamicHttpSource.resolveUrl` now delegates too — its local rule turned `//cdn.example.com/1.jpg` into `https://base/cdn.example.com/1.jpg`, a well-formed URL pointing at a host that does not exist, which is *worse* than a rejected one because a real request is spent on it. **Both readers were affected and neither was the cause:** the fault was in the shared `HttpPageLoader`, and `ZoomableMangaPage`/`ComposeWebtoonReader` render the same `ReaderPageErrorView`. **The obvious alternative was rejected on evidence:** rebuilding each URL through `HttpUrl` would canonicalise signed CDN URLs, whose exact spelling *is* the credential `DEF-020` depends on. Absolute URLs are therefore returned byte-identical, and idempotence is pinned because the rule is applied at more than one boundary and the disk-cache key must not depend on how many times a URL passed through. **Root-relative, not RFC 3986 — corrected by running the tests, not by reading the code.** I expected `/1.jpg` against a base with a path to resolve to the origin and wrote that assertion; it failed, and the implementation was right. `getUrlWithoutDomain` stores a manga/chapter URL as `uri.path` and `pageListRequest` rebuilds it as `baseUrl + chapter.url`, so a leading `/` already means "relative to `baseUrl`" throughout the source layer — resolving it against the origin would make page resolution disagree with chapter resolution for exactly the sources whose `baseUrl` carries a path. Two further assertions of mine were wrong the same way (`./data/1.jpg`, `data/../1.jpg` are joined, not canonicalised) and all three are now pinned with the reasoning attached. **Falsified:** neutering `resolve` to return its input turns **15 of 63** red across both `ImageUrlPolicy*Test` classes. **Evidence:** `:core:common`'s 63 tests were first compiled and run on a standalone JVM (JDK 21 / kotlinc 2.1.0 against `okhttp-jvm-5.5.0`) because this workspace has no Android SDK, and the reported exception was **reproduced verbatim** from the real `Requests.GET` for all three relative shapes and shown eliminated after. `HttpSourceImageUrlRequestTest` and `HttpPageLoaderUrlResolutionTest` **were subsequently run in CI and are green** (run `36746376376`, `c7f9a42e`). No device claim is made. |
| DEF-028 | **The retry decision existed in two modules and had drifted three ways, so a chapter could read successfully and fail to download.** Found 2026-09-30 while asking whether the reader was handling a source that hands back a *different* CDN on each resolution sanely. It was — and the investigation found the surrounding story was not. | `PageLoadRecoveryTest` (18 methods / 30 cases) for the decision, budget, jitter and history; `HttpPageLoaderCdnSwapTest` for the swap itself, which had **no** coverage of any kind; `PageLoadRecoveryStructuralTest` (4) for the downloader wiring and the no-second-schedule rule; `TransientErrorsTest` extended for the host-fault family | **CODE_COMPLETE at E2, falsification-verified, and now run in CI** (run `36746376376`, `c7f9a42e` — this row originally said the reader and downloader suites were written but not run, which was true when written and stopped being true the moment `main` built them). **The swap worked; the story around it did not.** The reader did drop a URL the classifier indicted and re-resolve, so a second CDN was picked up. Three things were wrong around it. **(1) Only the reader could change hosts.** `Downloader.downloadImage`'s `retryWhen` asked the shared `TransientErrors` whether a `403` or an unresolvable host indicted the URL — and then re-requested the identical string. `DEF-021` shared the *classifier*; the *action* stayed in two copies, so any source with short-lived signed image URLs produced a chapter that reads and will not download. **(2) The backoff had drifted.** 1s/2s/4s in the reader against `(2L shl attempt) * 1000` = 2s/4s/8s in the downloader. One rule, two schedules. **(3) Neither had jitter, and the only jittered backoff in the app covers the wrong failures.** `RateLimitBackoffInterceptor` jitters per host, and its own comment says why — but only for 429/503, while the URL-stale family is 403 and dead hosts. Pages that failed together retried together. **The host-at-fault family was half-detected.** `shouldReResolveUrl` knew `UnknownHostException` and not the failures that say the same thing about a *host*: `SSLPeerUnverifiedException`, `SSLHandshakeException`, `ConnectException`. Each is an `IOException`, so each was already being retried — four times, against the identical URL, for a host a fresh resolution could have replaced. **Timeouts deliberately excluded:** nothing portable separates a connect-phase timeout from a slow read, and a slow read is not evidence about the host. **Fix: one owner.** `PageLoadRecovery` decides and does not act — dropping the URL and calling `getImageUrl` stay with the caller, which owns the page and the source, and pulling them in would need a `Source` in `core:common`. It holds the decision, the budget, the jittered backoff, the memory of a structurally-unusable URL, and the attempt history. **The budget is deliberately one counter and deliberately unchanged at three retries.** A larger allowance for re-resolves is tempting, since only the URL changes; it would raise the worst-case requests per page, so only the *delay* differs by kind (250ms vs 1s — a re-resolve is a different request to a possibly different host, and waiting as long as a same-URL retry is the wrong trade). `ReResolvePacer` spaces re-resolutions per loader, because sources mint signed URLs in batches with a common expiry and a chapter opened near that boundary has every page fail at once. **Diagnosis, which did not exist:** `summary()` yields `tried 3 attempt(s): cdn-a.example (HTTP 403), cdn-b.example (timeout), cdn-c.example (HTTP 403)`, logged where each caller gives up. The `DEF-027` report had to be reasoned about from first principles because nothing recorded which hosts had been tried. **The swap had no coverage, and that is the finding behind this row.** `PageRetryUrlPolicyStructuralTest` asserts *textually* that the loader asks the classifier, and states in its own docstring that it does not prove a page recovers. So the code read as though the swap worked and nothing had ever watched it. `HttpPageLoaderCdnSwapTest` now drives the real ladder through a source handing out a sequence of hosts and asserts the **sequence of URLs requested** — plus the counterweights: a dropped connection must *not* spend a resolution, a `404` must not be retried, and a page that exhausts its hosts must fail **holding no URL**, or that URL reaches the persisted page list and the next open starts with it. **The checks caught two things in this change rather than after it.** The budget gate first matched `shl attempt`/`shl retries` — the exact expressions that had drifted — and a probe writing a fresh `(2L shl 1) * 1000` passed straight through; it now matches the property (computing a delay), and three probes confirm it bites. The same gate's first version matched a *comment* in `Downloader` quoting the old code, which would have left it permanently red against correct source; it strips comments first. **Also fixed here:** the pager's error view renders `RateLimitedRetry` for 429/503, which webtoon already did for the identical failure from the identical shared loader — the pager was the one reader that let a user tap Retry into a source that had just asked to be left alone. `isRateLimitError` was already pure and tested; only the wiring was missing. **Evidence:** `:core:common` **103 tests compiled and run** on a standalone JVM (JDK 21 / kotlinc 2.1.0 vs `okhttp-jvm-5.5.0`) — green — and falsified four ways: removing the `ConnectException` arm, removing the jitter, raising `DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES` 3→5, and neutering `resolve` in `DEF-027`. **The budget probe found a test that asserted nothing:** it built a *configured* instance, so raising the *shipped* default left the suite green. There is now a separate assertion through the no-argument constructor, because a test that cannot fail when its policy changes is not a test of that policy. The four structural gates were verified by re-implementing their matchers and running them against the tree (0 offenders / 139 files) and against three deliberate violations (1 offender each). **A regression this row's own first fix introduced, caught by review before it shipped, and recorded because the sequence is the point.** The first wiring paced *every* URL resolution rather than only re-resolutions, putting up to 400ms onto the load of any page -- including the one the user is waiting for, which competes with the preload window for the same 1-3 workers. Measured against the real `ReResolvePacer` on a six-page preload window: **1.2s of added latency, 400ms worst single page**, paid in the *normal* case to solve a *failure-path* problem. The line looks harmless in review and nothing about it is wrong until measured, which is why `PageLoadRecovery.isRetrySequence` now gates it, `ReResolvePacer.paceReResolution` is named for what it is, and a structural gate fails the build if either module paces without that guard (verified against the tree and against the exact regression restored). Re-measured after the fix: **0ms on chapter open**, 400ms per re-resolution retained where it belongs. A second regression went with it: on the final attempt the downloader re-resolved *before* checking the budget, spending a source round-trip on a URL it was about to discard; the drop now precedes the give-up check and the re-resolution follows it. "

"**Also fixed on review: a replacement URL that could not be obtained replaced the real error.** A source that fills `Page.imageUrl` in `pageListParse` and never implements `imageUrlParse` throws the base `UnsupportedOperationException` when asked again -- arriving with no URL in hand, and becoming the message the user saw, in place of the `403` that had actually stopped their page. `onFailure`'s `null` URL is now the signal that a failure happened while *resolving* rather than while loading: only a load failure becomes the error surfaced on giving up, and the resolution failure stays in the history. Three tests, and it repairs the reader too, which had carried this since `DEF-020`. "
"**Jitter narrowed to half-again** rather than double. Any spread breaks the lockstep, so the wider window bought nothing and cost up to a second on the first retry; the floor is still the un-jittered schedule, so jitter can only ever make a caller wait longer than before, never less. "
"**A third review, on the user's side of the system, found a status that was confidently wrong and a Retry button that could be 18 seconds away.** The page is behind a spinner for the whole ladder and the Retry button only exists in the error state, so the ladder's length *is* how long the user is left with no way to intervene. Three findings: **`page.progress` was never reset by the reader** -- it is written by `ProgressListener` as bytes arrive, so a download that died at 47% left the retry ladder showing a **frozen determinate spinner at 47%** (`Downloader` already reset it; the reader did not). **The ladder was unbounded in wall-clock, and the jitter change had lengthened it.** **`RateLimitedRetry` held its countdown in `remember(page)`**, and page identity is stable, so the cooldown was spent once and every later rate-limited failure offered an already-enabled button -- exactly backwards, since tapping straight back into a rate-limited source is as true on the fifth failure as the first. All three fixed; the countdown is now keyed on the `Page.State.Error` instance, which is fresh per failure.
**`PageLoadRecovery` gained an elapsed bound (5s), armed by the *first* failure** so a legitimately slow first download with a real progress bar does not count against the user -- only our own invisible retrying does. **What it does and does not bound, measured rather than assumed** (1.5s per request): connection reset 14.7s -> 8.2s; slow CDN at 4s/request with a dead host 18.2s -> 12.9s; **403 re-resolving 8.2s -> 8.2s, unchanged**. That last row is the honest cost of protecting the CDN swap: a re-resolving ladder retries so fast that the *attempt* budget binds first, and four cheap attempts at a possibly different host beat three. The bound truncates our retrying, not the source's response time, because aborting an in-flight transfer would discard one about to succeed -- so it is a ceiling on our own behaviour, not a promise about how long anyone waits.
**The webtoon visibility watchdog now tests for a stale `Queue` positively** rather than for "not Ready". It was safe before only because `loadPage` declines to re-queue a page that is not `Queue` -- a load-bearing coincidence across two files, and it meant the watchdog triggered pointless neighbour preloads in the middle of a ladder.
**A latent trap found by a simulation rather than by reading:** the bound was first written as a precomputed deadline, and `now + Long.MAX_VALUE` wraps negative -- so "no bound" would have become "no retries at all" for any caller passing a large value. Now measured as elapsed-since-first-failure, which cannot overflow. Two tests of mine were also wrong in the same pass and the run caught both: the elapsed test exhausted the *attempt* budget before the clock could be the reason (so it passed for the wrong reason until the assertion on the reason text), and an off-by-one in the disabled-bound case.
**All suites now run in CI and are green** (run `36746376376`, `c7f9a42e`): `HttpPageLoaderCdnSwapTest` 7, `HttpPageLoaderUrlResolutionTest` 6, `PageLoadRecoveryStructuralTest` 5, plus `PageLoadRecoveryTest` and the `ImageUrlPolicy*`/`HttpSourceImageUrlRequestTest` suites in `core:common` and `source-api`. **What is still not claimed:** `Downloader` cannot be tested behaviourally on the JVM at all — it needs a `Context`, a `UniFile` root, a `WorkManager` and a live cache — so its half rests on the structural gates, and no device claim is made. **The three CI round-trips it took to get here were worth recording:** the first failed `spotlessCheck` and skipped the tests, the second reached `Build app` and then failed compiling the new reader test fakes (`supportsLatest` missing, a `delay` in a non-suspend function, a constructor parameter that needed to be a `val`), and the third on `String.count` in the structural gate. Every one was a type error invisible to a JVM-only harness, and two of the three would have been caught by compiling those files standalone — the structural gate needed only JUnit and a JDK-only helper, which had been assumed otherwise. | `01f706c`, `f121ebc` |

Legacy defects are not fixed by changing the current implementation unless the task explicitly says so. They are acceptance inputs for the replacement.

### Evidence obligations transfer to the replacement

`DEF-001`, `DEF-002`, `DEF-003`, `DEF-005` and `DEF-008` are fixed in code inside the **legacy** viewer
monoliths (`ZoomableMangaPage`, `ComposeWebtoonReader`, `ComposePagerReader`, and the shared-element
motion policy). `CLEAN-001` deletes those files.

> **Correction (2026-11-07, A6/D2):** this was written before the reader's architecture settled, and it is no longer true. Those files are not a legacy generation — they *are* the production Compose readers (the adopted Mihon 0.21.1 viewer shells host them), A6 verified that no superseded reader path ships beside them, and `CLEAN-001`'s executed scope is recorded in its ledger row. The evidence-obligation rule below still holds: the obligations transfer to whichever file owns the behaviour.

**The acceptance obligations therefore transfer with the behaviour, not with the file.** When
`RDR-004`/`RDR-005` replace the paged and continuous readers, each defect's required evidence
applies to the **replacement** viewport:

| Defect | Must be re-proven against | At cutover |
|---|---|---|
| `DEF-001` | the paged replacement's zoom transform | `RDR-004` |
| `DEF-002` | the continuous replacement's isotropic document zoom | `RDR-005` |
| `DEF-003` | the continuous replacement's one-transform/one-clip surface | `RDR-005` |
| `DEF-005` | the single-graph shell's shared-element motion | `NAV-001` |
| `DEF-008` | the media render path's crop policy | `MED-001` consumer |

**This is recorded so deletion cannot quietly discharge it.** Deleting the legacy fix without
transferring the evidence would leave a user-reported defect permanently unproven while every ledger
row reads `CODE_COMPLETE` â€” the exact shape of the failure this programme has already produced once,
where an absence of evidence was mistaken for evidence. A row may not be closed by the deletion of
the code that implemented it.

| B-047 | **`TST-001C3` is closed, and the long-standing "no .jxl fixture exists anywhere" blocker turned out to be unnecessary.** 2026-09-27. The premise behind that blocker was that a JXL fixture had to be sourced from outside the project. It does not: the app already bundles `jxl-coder`, and the same library that decodes can **encode**, so the artifact under test is produced by the code that will read it back. That is stronger than an imported file, because a hand-placed artifact can be stale relative to the decoder, whereas this one cannot be. `JxlCoilBridgeDeviceTest` (7) encodes a known 64x96 image, asserts `isJxl()` accepts it, decodes through the exact `JxlCoder.decodeSampled` call the bridge makes, and checks geometry, corner colours, the sampled-size branch, and that a real JPEG is *not* accepted. **Two things this exercise found, both of which would have shipped as false confidence:** (1) **the default encode is lossy** â€” the first run failed with `expected -65536 but was -60928`, i.e. red 255 came back as 238. That is correct codec behaviour and a wrong assertion, so the exact-pixel test now encodes with `JxlCompressionOption.LOSSLESS` and a separate case pins the lossy path with an approximate-colour assertion. (2) **The encoder emits a bare codestream (`FF 0A`), not a container.** Reintroducing the original `0x4A` box-type defect left the whole suite **green**, because the bare-codestream branch of `isJxl()` short-circuits before the container check. So the branch the original defect actually broke had *no* device coverage at all, and the test that looked like it closed `TST-001C3` would have certified a path it never exercised. Two container tests were added; reintroducing the defect now turns **2 of 12** red. That is the falsification that distinguishes this from the earlier version, and it is the reason the row can claim `E3`. `core:data` needed `androidTestImplementation(libs.jxl.coder.coil)`: the `api` declaration covers main and unit tests but not the instrumentation compilation. |
| B-048 | **`DEF-001` device evidence was flaky on a software-rendered emulator, and the E3 tests never ran in CI at all.** Found 2026-09-27 while triaging the `Build & Test` failure. Two separate problems, both invisible until the gate was made to report. **(1) The instrumentation gate covered one test in `:app` and ran weekly.** `connected-instrumentation.yml` booted an emulator but invoked only `:app:connectedDebugAndroidTest` and only on a cron schedule, so the 18 device tests in `core:data` and `feature:reader` â€” every `E3` claim this programme makes â€” were **never executed by CI at all**. They were reproducible only on the machine that wrote them. Now triggered on push and pull_request, scoped to paths that can affect a device test, and extended to all three modules. **(2) `aRealPinchChangesTheRenderedPage` failed with `Failed waiting for PixelCopy!`.** `captureToImage` is implemented over `PixelCopy`; the existing `settleFrame()` guarantees a frame *exists*, but on a software renderer (CI uses `-gpu swiftshader_indirect`, this machine does not) the copy itself can exceed Compose wait. Four sibling capture tests pass on the same emulator and only the one capturing *after an injected gesture* fails, which is what identifies it as a timing fault rather than a capability limit. Fixed by retrying **the capture only**, up to four attempts, and re-throwing any error that is not a PixelCopy timeout. A wrong image still fails the scale assertion exactly as before, and exhaustion raises a message saying the assertions never ran â€” a retry that could hide a real defect would be worse than the flake. |
| B-049 | **The CI artifact globs never matched a nested module, and my first fix of them did not either.** 2026-09-27. The `Connected Instrumentation` run on `main` failed and still uploaded **zero** artifacts after I had already widened the path from `app/build/...` to `*/build/...`. The reason is that in GitHub's glob syntax a single `*` does not cross a path separator, so `*/build/...` matches `app/build/...` and `core/build/...` but **not** `core/data/build/...`, which is where the JXL and border-crop results live. `**` is required. The same defect was in `build.yml`, which I had widened the same wrong way. Corrected in both, with the reason recorded inline so the next edit does not reintroduce it. The underlying lesson is the one from `B-047`: widening a path is not the same as making it match, and the only way to tell the difference is to look at where the files actually are -- `core/data` and `core/download` are nested one level deeper than the module the original path named. |
| B-050 | **The connected instrumentation gate cannot run on GitHub runners: the API 35 emulator does not boot, so it fails before any test executes.** 2026-09-27. Found by reading the step conclusions rather than the job verdict -- `Connected Instrumentation` run #1 failed, and the failing step is `Boot API 35 emulator`, with `Summarise instrumentation failures` reporting no recorded failure because no test had run. The same provisioning failure is what has failed all 24 scheduled macrobenchmark runs (`B-043`), on a different runner (`macos-14`), so this is a capability gap rather than one workflow's configuration. **This row exists because of a mistake worth recording:** having just closed the gap that the 18 E3 device tests never ran in CI, I broadened the workflow to trigger on push and pull_request. That was the wrong call. A gate that cannot run, wired to every push, marks every commit red for a reason unrelated to the code, and trains everyone to ignore red -- after which a genuine failure is invisible too. That is the inert-gate failure mode with a different hat, and it is worse than having no gate. The trigger has been reverted to weekly plus manual, with the reasoning left in the workflow so the next person sees why it is not "obviously missing". The multi-module coverage, the failure summariser and the corrected artifact globs all stay, ready for the day the emulator boots. **ACCEPTED BY OWNER 2026-09-28 â€” device runs stay local; CI emulator provisioning declined.** The failure is a runner capability gap, not a code defect: the failing step is `Boot API 35 emulator`, and the failure summariser reports nothing because no test ran. The same provisioning failure caused all 24 scheduled macrobenchmark runs on a different runner, so it is a capability gap rather than one workflow's configuration. **The decision:** keep the multi-module coverage, the failure summariser and the corrected artifact globs; leave the trigger at weekly plus manual; do not attempt to provision emulators. **Why this is honest rather than a gate quietly removed:** `ADR-0009` defines `E4-lab` as agent-driven capture against an attached emulator, which is exactly what these runs are, so the evidence channel is intact â€” what is lost is *automated* re-verification of device tests on every push, and that is a real reduction in coverage, stated here and in `TST-003` rather than dressed up. **The regression this row already caused is why the trigger stays weekly:** when the workflow was briefly wired to every push, a gate that cannot run marked every commit red for a reason unrelated to the code, which trains everyone to ignore red â€” after which a genuine failure is invisible too. That is the inert-gate failure mode with a different hat, and it is worse than having no gate. **Standing instruction for future work:** because device tests do not run in CI, any change touching `feature/reader/src/androidTest` or `core/data/src/androidTest` must run `:core:data:connectedDebugAndroidTest :feature:reader:connectedDebugAndroidTest` locally on `emulator-5554` before it is called done. | ACCEPTED â€” local-only device runs; `E4-lab` per ADR-0009; CI provisioning declined |
| B-051 | **Macrobenchmark is dropped by owner decision, 2026-09-27, and `TST-003` is completed against a scope that can actually be met.** `MacrobenchmarkRule` refuses to measure on an unrooted emulated device, so `.github/workflows/benchmark.yml` had failed all 24 of its scheduled runs and had never produced a result on any runner. It could not be fixed without a rooted image or physical hardware -- that is a cost, not an effort -- so it was a gate whose only possible state was red. Continuing to fund it was chasing a path that does not work, and its 24 red runs had been sitting in the scheduled list since 2026-09-18 as noise nobody acted on. The workflow is deleted. Of the ten budgets in `PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md`, **eight never depended on it**; the two that did are replaced rather than abandoned: frame deadline becomes a manual `E4-user` capture, and startup becomes a scripted `adb shell am start -W` `TotalTime`. **The cost of this decision, stated plainly:** the frame-deadline budget is now measured by nothing automatic, which is a genuine reduction in coverage. It is written into the budget table as "no automated gate" rather than quietly left looking covered. The `macrobenchmark` module itself remains in the build, unwired; retiring it is legacy deletion and belongs to `CLEAN-001`, so it is recorded there rather than half-removed here. **Sequencing consequence:** `TST-003` was the last thing preventing Phase 2 from closing, and Phase 2 gates Phase 3, which gates every phase after it. | SEC-002 | Encrypt source/tracker credentials with Keystore-backed storage. | Agent | `AndroidKeyStoreCipherDeviceTest` (1, `E3`, falsification-verified on API 37) + 8 JVM tests | **`CODE_COMPLETE at E3` â€” the round trip is proven on a device.** `TrackPreferences.trackToken` and `trackPassword` are the single choke point every tracker routes through, and both wrote **plaintext** strings to the preference store. The `privateKey` prefix only keeps them out of backups; the bytes on disk were still readable. The earlier note that this "needs a new dependency" was wrong â€” Android's platform `android.security.keystore` is available from API 23, so the Android adapter needs no new edge. The contract wraps the existing `Preference` rather than replacing the store, so one wrapper covers every credential and no call site changes shape. An **empty value deletes the entry** rather than storing an encrypted empty string, so absence has one representation. A credential that **cannot be decrypted is dropped, not thrown**: keys are invalidated by device-lock changes and are absent on any device a backup was restored to, so throwing would make the app unlaunchable after a restore. **Falsified:** making `set` store in the clear turned 3 of the 8 tests red. `AndroidKeyStoreCipher` uses AES-256/GCM with a **fresh IV per encryption** â€” GCM IV reuse under one key is catastrophic â€” and stores `base64(iv):base64(ciphertext)`. It requires **no user authentication**, deliberately: tracker tokens are refreshed by background work, and a lock-gated key would break that and push users toward a weaker unlock configuration. Wired in `AppModule` as a `@Singleton` `SecretCipher`, so production always supplies one. **`E3` evidence:** `AndroidKeyStoreCipherDeviceTest` writes a canary, proves the value round trips, then reads the persisted `.preferences_pb` as **raw bytes** and asserts the plaintext appears in neither Latin-1 nor UTF-16. **Falsified:** substituting an identity cipher turns it red with `the plaintext must not appear in the persisted bytes`, so it genuinely detects unencrypted storage rather than passing vacuously. It uses a dedicated DataStore file because DataStore refuses two active instances on one path and the running app already holds the real one; the code path exercised is identical. **Not yet observed:** a live tracker sign-in, and recovery after a real device-lock change invalidates the key. |

Publish paths therefore **consume** the verdict: `release.yml` gains a `gates` job its `build` job needs (a tag on a red or never-built commit fails before anything is signed), and `nightly.yml` gates its publish identically (a nightly is an installable publish to a release channel), both through `.github/actions/require-green-checks/action.yml`, which polls the shipped SHA's check runs and fails unless every check is completed and green and the named core gates (`Quality Checks`, `Build & Test App`) are present; the calling run's own jobs are excluded so a gate never waits on itself, and a commit reporting no checks at all fails closed after a bounded grace as never-verified. Instrumentation is deliberately not faked: connected instrumentation stays `B-050`-blocked (the API 35 emulator does not boot on GitHub runners) and is never wired into publish; benchmark/screenshot channels stay manual per `B-051`/D2. Held structurally by `ReleaseGateTest` (6: non-bypassable, nightly same verdict, single implementation, no emulator gate on publish paths, artifact gates present, accounted-for), with `app/build.gradle.kts` declaring `.github/actions` as a repository-file gate input so the suite reruns when the action changes. **Honesty:** no JDK locally; remote CI is the verification channel; the tag-triggered run is owner-side, pending the next `v*` tag; the nightly-path gate executes on the merge commit. | CODE_COMPLETE at E2 — gates structural and green; the tag-path run is pending the next `v*` tag (owner-side). |
| CLEAN-001 | Delete superseded reader/navigation/media code. | Agent | Dependency/import scan | **CODE_COMPLETE 2026-11-07 (D2).** The deletion happened at the cutovers, not here: reader at the A1—A6 cutovers (A6's ceiling-0 scan enumerated the 83-type inventory and the per-file audit found no superseded path), navigation at `NAV-001` (the two global mutable navigation singletons) and the `ADR-0011` re-scope, media at `MED-004`/`ADR-0010` — so D2's dependency/import scan found no reader/navigation/media generation left to delete. Executed here is the `B-051` scope extension: the `macrobenchmark` module (6 files), its `ephyra.benchmark` convention plugin, the app `benchmark` build type, and the unused `benchmark-macro`/`test-uiautomator` catalog entries are deleted; module count 29 → 28; no project edges change (nothing depended on `:macrobenchmark`). Build green on CI is the gate. |

| 2026-09-30 | **`DEF-028` added and fixed: the retry decision lived in two modules and had drifted three ways, so a chapter could read and fail to download.** Asked whether the reader was handling a source that returns a *different* CDN on each resolution sanely. The swap itself was fine — and the audit around it found: **(1)** only the reader could change hosts, because `Downloader`'s `retryWhen` consulted the shared classifier and then re-requested the identical URL anyway (`DEF-021` shared the classification; the action stayed duplicated); **(2)** the two backoffs had drifted to 1s/2s/4s against 2s/4s/8s; **(3)** neither had jitter, and the app's only jittered backoff covers 429/503 rather than the 403-and-dead-host family. `TransientErrors` also gained the host-at-fault failures it was missing — `SSLPeerUnverifiedException`, `SSLHandshakeException`, `ConnectException` — each of which was being retried four times against a host a re-resolve could have replaced. Timeouts excluded on purpose: nothing portable separates a connect-phase timeout from a slow read. **One owner now:** `PageLoadRecovery` decides and does not act, so the page and the source stay with the caller. **The request budget is deliberately unchanged at three retries** — a bigger allowance for re-resolves is tempting and would raise the worst-case requests per page, so only the delay differs by kind. `ReResolvePacer` spaces re-resolutions per loader, because sources mint signed URLs in batches with a common expiry and a chapter opened near the boundary has every page fail at once. **The finding behind the row is a coverage gap:** the swap had *no* test. `PageRetryUrlPolicyStructuralTest` checks textually that the loader asks the classifier and says outright that it does not prove a page recovers — so the code read as though the swap worked and nothing had watched it. `HttpPageLoaderCdnSwapTest` now asserts the **sequence of URLs requested**, plus the counterweights (a dropped connection must not spend a resolution; a `404` must not be retried; an exhausted page must fail holding no URL, or that URL is persisted and re-requested next open). **Diagnosis now exists:** `summary()` produces `tried 3 attempt(s): cdn-a.example (HTTP 403), cdn-b.example (timeout) …`, logged where each caller gives up. The `DEF-027` report had to be reconstructed from first principles because nothing recorded what had been tried. Also: the pager now shows the rate-limit countdown retry that webtoon already had for the identical shared-loader failure. **Two things the checks caught in-flight:** the no-second-schedule gate first matched the two expressions that had already drifted, and a probe writing a fresh `(2L shl 1) * 1000` passed through it — it now matches the property, with three probes confirming it bites. The same gate's first version matched a *comment* quoting the old code, which would have left it permanently red against correct source. **Evidence:** `:core:common` **103 tests run** on a standalone JVM (no Android SDK here), green, falsified four ways. The budget probe found a test that asserted nothing — it built a configured instance, so raising the *shipped* default left the suite green; there is now an assertion through the no-argument constructor. The four structural gates were validated by re-running their matchers against the tree (0 offenders / 139 files) and three deliberate violations. **Review caught two regressions in this row's own first fix, both recorded rather than quietly repaired.** It paced *every* URL resolution rather than only re-resolutions: measured against the real pacer, **1.2s added to a six-page preload window and up to 400ms on the page the user is waiting for**, in the normal case, to solve a failure-path problem. And on the final attempt the downloader re-resolved before checking the budget, spending a source round-trip on a URL it was about to discard. Both fixed and now gated; re-measured at **0ms on chapter open**. Also repaired on review: a replacement URL that could not be obtained (a source filling `Page.imageUrl` in `pageListParse` never implements `imageUrlParse`, so asking again throws) was replacing the real `403` with `UnsupportedOperationException` in the message the user saw. A `null` URL in `onFailure` now means *failed while resolving*, and only a load failure is surfaced on giving up -- which repairs the reader too, where this had lived since `DEF-020`. Jitter narrowed to half-again: any spread breaks the lockstep, so the wider window bought nothing. **Not claimed:** the reader and downloader suites need Gradle and **have not been
| 2026-09-30 | **`DEF-027` added and fixed: a page whose URL the source named relatively could not be requested at all, and the reader showed OkHttp's internal message.** Owner report: `Expected URL scheme 'http' or 'https' but no scheme was found for …` on a manga page, with the page live at the source and no rate limiting. That exception comes from `toHttpUrl()` while a `Request` is **built**, which is why the report's own facts corroborate rather than contradict it: nothing was sent, so no host was contacted and no limit applied. No relative page URL was absolutised anywhere — `getPages` copies `Page.url` verbatim, and this fork's `HttpSource.getImageUrl` is the deprecated *network* variant, so upstream's `baseUrl` concatenation is absent. `DEF-023`'s guard sits after `getImageUrl` and inspects `imageUrl`, never `Page.url`, and an `IllegalArgumentException` classifies as **permanent**, so there was no backoff ladder and Retry repeated the same inputs verbatim. **The owner's framing set the fix's shape: reduce errors that happen before a request is sent, rather than producing our own in their place.** So the fix is *resolution*, not another verdict — a relative URL is incomplete, not broken, and the missing half is a value the app already holds. **No new throw site was added.** `ImageUrlPolicy.resolve` is the single owner and is applied at every boundary that can reach a request (`HttpSource.imageUrlRequest`/`imageRequest`, the loader, and `Downloader`, which had no policy call at all); `DynamicHttpSource.resolveUrl` stops mangling `//cdn…` into a nonexistent host. **Rejected on evidence:** rebuilding URLs through `HttpUrl` would canonicalise signed CDN URLs, whose spelling *is* the `DEF-020` credential; absolute URLs are returned byte-identical and idempotence is pinned, because the rule runs at several boundaries and the disk-cache key must not depend on how many times a URL passed through. **The tests corrected the design, not just the assertions:** I asserted `/1.jpg` against a base with a path resolves to the origin (RFC 3986). It does not, the implementation was right, and the reason is that `getUrlWithoutDomain`/`pageListRequest` already read a leading `/` as "relative to `baseUrl`" — so origin-resolution would have made page resolution disagree with chapter resolution for every source whose `baseUrl` carries a path. **Evidence, with its limits stated:** `:core:common`'s **63 tests were compiled and run** on a standalone JVM (JDK 21 / kotlinc 2.1.0 vs `okhttp-jvm-5.5.0`) since this workspace has no Android SDK, and the reported exception was **reproduced verbatim** from the real `Requests.GET` for all three relative shapes and shown eliminated. **Falsified:** neutering `resolve` turns **15 of 63** red. `HttpSourceImageUrlRequestTest` and `HttpPageLoaderUrlResolutionTest` need Gradle/AGP and were not run at this row's date; both **are run and green in CI now** (`36746376376`). No device claim. | `e8980ed`, `eef62f4` |

| 2026-09-28 | **P0 batch 1 â€” four reader-reliability defects fixed, all on the "missed images" causal chain.** `DEF-018` **(critical):** a page evicted from the disk cache was reset to `Queue` and thrown, but **`Queue` is a status, not an enqueue** â€” nothing put it back on the priority queue, the pager's only trigger is `LaunchedEffect(page)` keyed on page *identity* so it cannot re-fire on a status change, and the worker only takes entries actually queued. The eviction branch now resets *and* re-offers at the priority it was dequeued at, so it re-enters circulation without jumping the preload window. `DEF-019` **(high):** `ChapterCache.fetchAndCacheImage` returned silently when `openEditor()` yielded null. The two cases are now distinguished by probing for an existing snapshot. `DEF-020` **(high):** the reader's `isTransient` predicate treated `403` as permanent, which for a signed or time-limited image URL is exactly backwards â€” the URL is stale, the source will issue a different one â€” so the page burned the full 1s/2s/4s backoff ladder and then failed permanently. It cannot, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of remaining effort. `Application.attachBaseContext` and `onCreate` are **Android lifecycle callbacks, not coroutine-dispatched work.** The reducer's model is `reduce(state, intent) -> transition(state, effects)`, where each effect is *dispatched* and its result is fed back as the next intent. The remaining gates are structural only`ModuleDependencyGraphTest` (6 layering rules), `SigningSecretTest` (4), `ManifestPrivilegeTest` (4), plus domain-purity and no-`data.*`-in-features CI greps â€” because a structural rule cannot be satisfied by editing a number and a count always can. Size metrics are now schedule-measured and recorded in [`BUILD_HEALTH.md`](BUILD_HEALTH.md). Added **ADR-0009** and redefined the evidence ladder by *producing system*: `E3` is instrumentation/screenshot/pointer-event on an emulator, **`E4-user` is validation on real user devices from a pushed build**, `E4-lab` is in-tree device capture and is optional. This project validates by pushing a build and exercising it on real devices; the old ladder demanded in-tree agent-captured `E4` artifacts for behaviour that exists in no shipped build until it is wired, which is a standing invitation to fabricate. Added the two rules that close that: **a missing channel is `BLOCKED`, never `pass`**, and **a record may only describe a run that occurred** â€” a reproduction that cannot be re-performed is void regardless of outcome. The five `DEF` rows now declare their channel instead of saying "E4 pending". `E4_ACCEPTANCE.md` gained an `E4-user` validation-record format and states that `E4-lab` availability never blocks an `E4-user` row. Also removed the ROADMAP and execution-guide instructions to never raise a ceiling, which contradicted the same programme's instruction to raise `mainSourceFiles` as contracts were added. | `HealthRatchetTest`/`HealthBaseline`/`health-baseline.json` deleted; ADR-0009 added; `:core:domain:testDebugUnitTest`, `:app:testDebugUnitTest`, `:app:assembleDebug` green; 14 structural gates verified on a forced re-run |
| 2026-09-26 | **Health metrics demoted, gates made falsifiable, and `.kilo` removed. No production behaviour changed.** Four things, in the order they were discovered. **(1) A measurement is a metric until it names an antipattern.** `BUILD_HEALTH.md` now states the promotion rule explicitly â€” a number becomes a gate only when it measures a violation to ratchet down *and* can be asserted structurally â€” and counts are recorded as informational. This replaces the previous "structural or unwanted regression" phrasing, which had already been applied in practice but not stated as a bar. **(2) `ModuleDependencyGraphTest` could not fail.** The falsification probe â€” stage a deliberate `core â†’ feature` edge, expect red â€” came back *green*. Two defects: the extractor regex `projects\.([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9_]*)` captured one segment from `projects.feature.reader` and a `startsWith(":")` filter then discarded it, so all 166 real edges were dropped and `allEdges()` was always empty; separately, prefix filters compared `startsWith("core")` against `:core:domain`, which the leading colon made unmatchable. Five of six rules were asserting over an empty list. The gate had been reported as the structural backstop for layering since `ARC-001`. Found by probing, not by reading. **(3) The debt it was hiding is now quantified** â€” `B-020`: 3 `core:domain` project edges and 24 feature-to-feature edges, declared individually with the same two-way staleness check `SEC-003` applies to manifest permissions, so a new edge fails and a retired one must be deleted from the declaration. A count could not have named which edge to remove. Three self-tests were added for the extractor, the layer matcher, and a non-empty graph, so a future regression cannot silently restore the no-op. **(4) Two structural blind spots closed.** The tree walks in `ModuleDependencyGraphTest` and `SigningSecretTest` moved from `File.walkTopDown()` plus an exclusion list to `git ls-files`; such a list fails *open*, and `SigningSecretTest` is the sharp case, since a keystore in an unscanned tree is exactly the leak it exists to catch. And `ci.yml` had `paths: ['!**.md', '!docs/**']`, so a markdown-only PR ran no CI at all â€” in a program that treats documentation as a deliverable, that made the documentation gate structurally unfalsifiable. Finally, `.kilo/` and its worktree copy were removed: the worktree held zero unique commits and sat five behind `main`, but it was a full second copy of the repository inside the working tree and had already caused one false measurement that led to a ceiling being raised in error. Also corrected `FIXTURE_MANIFEST.md`, which still described a single `E4` as "representative device" and so contradicted ADR-0009, and recorded `B-021`: no fixture data exists in any shippable source set, so no reader `E4-user` claim is currently producible. | `ModuleDependencyGraphTest` (11), `SigningSecretTest` (4), `ManifestPrivilegeTest` (4) â€” 19 green; falsification probe confirmed red on a staged violation and the probe was removed; `:app:testDebugUnitTest` |
| 2026-09-26 | **The continuous reader cannot repeat the paged cutover, and that is now recorded rather than discovered later.** `RDR-004` stage 1 went smoothly, so the same shape was attempted for `RDR-005` and stopped: the shipping continuous reader uses `WebtoonDocumentZoom` (`scale` plus `offsetX`) and returns a `scrollCorrection`, because the `LazyColumn` owns vertical position, while `DocumentViewport` carries `offset.y` and clamps both axes with no concept of an external scroll owner. Wiring the target model in would have given the surface two owners of vertical position, which would fight and surface as content that jumps on the exact surface where `DEF-002` and `DEF-003` are meant to improve. Recorded as `B-025` with the decision that `WebtoonDocumentZoom` remains correct for a `LazyColumn`-backed surface and `DocumentViewport` is the canvas-surface model, so no future pass wires the incompatible one in. The same reasoning is now in `DOCUMENT_VIEWPORT_CONTRACT.md`, which previously presented `DocumentViewport` as unconditionally the target, and the `RDR-005` row is `IN_PROGRESS` blocked rather than unwired. Nothing was cut over and no half-finished work was left in the tree. | `DocumentViewport.kt`, `WebtoonDocumentZoom.kt`; `:app`, `:feature:reader`, `:core:domain` suites green |

| 2026-11-06 | **A2 landed: `ReaderSession` is production-wired through `ReaderSessionCoordinator` (RDR-001), and the reader's prev/next window is selected through the domain `ReaderChapterWindowPolicy` (RDR-002).** `ReaderViewModel` now owns a `ReaderSessionCoordinator` whose effect handler, `ReaderSessionEffectLog`, *acknowledges* effects rather than executing them — each effect names work the dispatching path already performs, and re-issuing would duplicate loads, persists and prefetches; the log is the seam the replacement resource owners delegate through when they land. | 2026-11-06 | **A3 landed: `StartupReducer` is production-wired onto `App.kt` through `StartupWorkflowHost.kt`, and `RECONCILE_SOURCES` — the step the `B-034` audit found absent — has an implementation (`SourceReconciler.kt`).** The 2026-09-28 entry below said `App` is the wrong host for this reducer, and the structural reason was real: lifecycle callbacks are not dispatch loops, and the critical phases rethrow so the process dies rather than launching half-initialised. | 2026-11-08 | **Reader progress recorder, CI round 2: the round-1 fix push itself introduced the violation that failed this round - a staging-newline bug in the author's tooling, disclosed as an authoring defect.** Round 2 (the round-1 fix head) failed `Quality Checks` at the `Check code format` step again. This round the diagnosis is log-evidenced, not source review: a temporary diagnose workflow (pushed to the branch, deleted before merge, commits left in PR history) ran `spotlessCheck` and re-emitted the violation hunks as check-run annotations; they named exactly one violation - the recorder test now ended with a blank line after its final closing brace (`}` + two newlines at EOF), a `ktlint` final-newline-class violation the branch had never had before the fix push. Root cause, stated plainly: the fix commit's test file was staged by extracting the fetched resource text with `jq -r`, which appends exactly one newline to extracted content, and the pre-push fidelity check asserted only that the file ends with a newline - which a doubled trailing newline also satisfies - so the wrapped-assertion fix was correct but its delivery appended a blank line, and the branch's own format gate caught it one round later. The diagnose workflow's own first version carried an authoring defect disclosed in the same spirit: its annotation emitter used line-oriented `sed 's/\n/%0A/g'`, which cannot substitute newlines, so the first diagnose round emitted one line per annotation and named nothing; the second version (`sed -z`) delivered the hunks that named this violation. Fixed: the test file's trailing blank line is removed - the file ends with `}` and one newline, byte-identical to the pre-fix branch content apart from the wrapped assertion - and the diagnose workflow is deleted in the same wave; the standing diagnosis channel for any future round is to push it again. **Honesty:** the code diff is one deleted blank line; no production code changed; the round-2 violation was author-introduced and is recorded as such rather than as a pre-existing defect; remote CI is the verification channel (no JDK locally); no device evidence claimed. | `ReaderProgressRecorderTest.kt` |
